campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:39: | [08 Bregan D'aerthe](08-bregan-daerthe.md) | Grand Game Stance, Quest Hooks, First Meeting, Renown & Ranks, Missions — and, as a villain faction: Response Teams, Outposts, Escalation, Revelation List |
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:30: | **Faction Outposts** | Harper informants can be activated against Xanathar and Splinter outposts (Wise Owl, Renown 25). One informant inside the Sea Maidens Faire reports Jarlaxle has been asking about the party. |
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:13: Alliance speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener. Jalester also separates the Splinter from Davil Starsong's cell, with the words "not Davil's people". They say "the carnival fleet" until **Jarlaxle Unmasked** is marked. They never say more of the Cassalanters than that the family is too rich and too pleasant, and nobody in the Alliance has proof of anything.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:34: | **Sea Maidens Faire** | Laeral wants the carnival fleet's private cargo manifests. If **Jarlaxle Unmasked** is marked, she wants to understand what Bregan D'aerthe is doing in Waterdeep. |
campaign/guides/factions/04-emerald-enclave.md:13: Enclave speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener. Kolat Towers stands one block north of Phaulkonmere, and nobody in the Enclave points at it before that gate. They never name the Cassalanters as anything worse than a rich house, and they never say "Jarlaxle" or "Bregan D'aerthe" to players.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:13: Order speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener, and Savra keeps Davil's Doom Raiders apart from the Splinter. They say "the carnival fleet" until **Jarlaxle Unmasked** is marked. Savra says "the family" until a player names the Cassalanters, and then she adds that she has no proof. Nobody in the Order knows what the family worships, and nobody names a pact.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:9: She learns the rest incrementally through the party. Mission 1 adds Hlam's warning about the buried thing waking. Mission 2 brings a report of a vessel fixed under the *Eyecatcher*'s hull, which is the Bregan D'aerthe submersible, and she never learns whose it is. By Mission 4 she knows where Nihiloor's pool lies, from one report by an agent inside the Guild who has not answered since. When the party delivers the full Grand Game picture, with all four factions, the Stone, the vault and the timeline, she goes still for a long time and then begins writing.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:11: Force Grey speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener, and they name Manshoon and Kolat Towers only after that. They never say "Jarlaxle" or "Bregan D'aerthe" to players.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:1: # Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:3: Game mechanics for running Bregan D'aerthe as both a player faction and a villain faction. For who they are, see the **Bregan D'aerthe** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:7: Jarlaxle's purpose in Waterdeep is intelligence — what the city's power structure looks like from the inside, which Masked Lords carry real weight with the Lords' Alliance council, and what it would take to buy Luskan a seat at that table. The half-million dragons in Neverember's vault are a welcome side opportunity. The political picture is the actual mission.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:9: Rather than competing with Xanathar or Manshoon for the Stone, Jarlaxle already holds Eye #3, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* and mounted beneath the *Eyecatcher*'s keel, and is content to let the other factions exhaust themselves chasing each other. His asset inside Xanathar's organization — **Nar'l Xibrindas**, a drow mage who serves as the beholder's advisor and reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell — keeps him informed of the Guild's movements. The Sea Maidens Faire parades give him cover to move personnel and materials through any ward without scrutiny.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:11: The vault itself, a way to open the Stone without all three Eyes, and leverage over Waterdeep's nobility independent of the carnival's goodwill — those are the gaps the party can fill, provided Jarlaxle decides to cultivate them.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:13: **What Bregan D'aerthe shares proactively:**
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:14: - Anonymous black coin pouches arrive at the party's door after Missions 1 and 3, unexplained. Amounts: 50 gp with no note, then 100 gp with a short note promising a more interesting assignment. Jarlaxle is establishing a pattern of generosity before he introduces himself.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:18: **What Bregan D'aerthe asks for:**
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:19: - Information about Waterdeep's political landscape — specifically, which Masked Lords carry the most weight with the Lords' Alliance council. The party can't gather this easily, but Jarlaxle is patient; he accumulates answers across missions without appearing to track them.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:20: - Discretion, applied aggressively. Bregan D'aerthe has operated in Waterdeep for months without the City Watch knowing drow are involved. A party that exposes the faction's drow composition ends the relationship immediately.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:21: - Competence. Jarlaxle offers more responsibility as the party demonstrates capability. The missions escalate because he is evaluating them for **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:23: Jarlaxle's Grand Game goal: hold Eye #3, collect political intelligence, keep all four villain factions sufficiently disrupted that none can move on the vault without the party's cooperation, position Bregan D'aerthe as the preferred **Sea Maidens Faire** ally, and use the vault gold — returned publicly to Waterdeep — to buy Lords' Alliance recognition of Luskan's reformed governance.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:27: | Quest | Bregan D'aerthe Hook |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:30: | **Gralhund Villa** | Jarlaxle (as J.B. Nevercott) was at the edge of the Gralhund Villa incident and saw which factions converged. He didn't intervene. Word comes through Krebbyg: "We saw what happened at Gralhund. We know who left with what. If the party is interested in what we saw, the theater has a back room." His price for the intelligence is a private conversation — not a mission, a meeting. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:31: | **Faction Outposts** | The Seven Masks Theater appears on every list of Dock Ward Bregan D'aerthe assets the party might assemble from outpost intelligence. At Renown 3+, Krebbyg has briefed the door staff: party members walk in without questions. At Renown 0, the theater is just a target on a list. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:32: | **Sea Maidens Faire** | Jarlaxle reveals himself. The caper heist path and the alliance path diverge here. Parties who completed Missions 4–5 have built enough operational trust that Jarlaxle offers the negotiation before the confrontation. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:33: | **Vault of Dragons** | At Dread Lord renown: Jarlaxle proposes returning the gold publicly, in the party's name, to Laeral Silverhand — in exchange for a letter of introduction to the Lords' Alliance council. This is what he has always wanted. The gold is the vehicle. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:37: Bregan D'aerthe recruits during **Trollskull Alley**. The offer extends to any party member. D'aerthe spies shadow the group for several days before contact; if the party reports the surveillance to the Watch, contact ends permanently. Otherwise, a haberdasher named J.B. Nevercott arrives at Trollskull Manor's front door, maintains his fiction until he judges himself secure, then names Bregan D'aerthe and offers a first small assignment. He leaves a black card with a silver ship.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:39: *Full write-up: [Bregan D'aerthe — First Meeting](../../quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md)*
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:45: - **Deliver intelligence that expands Jarlaxle's picture of the Grand Game (+1).** Any confirmed new information about a faction's Golorr artifact, lair location, or internal politics that he doesn't yet possess. Jarlaxle tells the character, with characteristic courtesy, if the information is already known.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:46: - **Acquire a Golorr artifact and deliver it to Jarlaxle (+2).** Delivering any Golorr artifact is the most direct service D'aerthe operatives can perform. Characters who negotiate terms before delivery may also extract additional personal benefits.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:47: - **Protect Nar'l Xibrindas's cover within Xanathar's Guild (+1).** Any action that deflects suspicion from Nar'l, discredits a threat to his position, or removes someone who has come close to identifying him earns D'aerthe renown — whether or not Jarlaxle explicitly requested it.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:49: - **Retrieve the nimblewright shipping records from the Sea Maidens Faire without tipping off Jarlaxle's crew (+2).** A successful heist on D'aerthe's own ships, which Jarlaxle discovers and appreciates as a demonstration of capability. Characters who pull this off earn renown regardless of whether they then join the faction; existing members earn Jarlaxle's genuine delight at being robbed by his own people.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:50: - **Impress Jarlaxle with unexpected creativity or competence (+1, once per quest).** Awarded when the party completes a mission or heist objective by a method its write-up doesn't list: a plan that is genuinely elegant, an improvisation that shouldn't have worked but did, a social maneuver that leaves everyone at the table better off.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:52: ### Bregan D'aerthe Ranks & Benefits
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:56: | 1 | **Initiate** | Recognized D'aerthe operative. Safe house access aboard the *Heartbreaker* or *Hellraiser*. Access to drow-made equipment at cost — hand crossbows, *+1 bolts*, darkvision-enhancing gear — through Fel'rekt Lafeen. All D'aerthe members extend professional courtesy: will not act against the character without direct orders from Jarlaxle. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:57: | 3 | **Soldier** | Jarlaxle shares one piece of intelligence per tenday drawn from D'aerthe's embedded network — including reports from Nar'l Xibrindas inside Xanathar's own council. Access to the *Sea Maidens Faire* for cover identity support and discreet meetings. Bregan D'aerthe fences: 20% discount on black-market purchases brokered through the company. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:58: | 10 | **Officer** | Jarlaxle assigns a drow **Spy** as a personal operative available between missions. Their loyalty is ultimately to Jarlaxle. The character gains access to D'aerthe's nimblewright shipping records — the only reliable means of tracing any construct sold in Waterdeep back to its buyer. One Uncommon magic item, personally selected by Jarlaxle because he has already read the character's intentions more clearly than they'd like. D'aerthe intelligence assessment on any faction, NPC, or location in Waterdeep on request — delivered within 48 hours, once per quest. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:59: | 25 | **Commander** | Jarlaxle accompanies the party on one mission per quest, fighting at full capacity. Two **Drow Gunslingers** and four **Drow** support one major operation per quest. Jarlaxle can arrange one of the following: a private audience with a Masked Lord, passage in or out of Waterdeep without City Watch knowledge, or the quiet disappearance of one named individual for six days. Once per quest. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:60: | 50 | **Houseless Noble** | Jarlaxle names the character an honorary member of Bregan D'aerthe's inner circle. Full Underdark network opens to them: contacts in Menzoberranzan, surface brokers from Luskan to Calimshan, and a standing invitation to operate under D'aerthe's protection anywhere in Faerûn. Access to the submarine *Scarlet Marpenoth* and its crew for any mission the character undertakes. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:67: | 3rd | **The Wazoo Affair** | Deliver an anonymous exposé on an unnamed Sea Ward family's hidden gold and missing servants to the publisher of *The Waterdeep Wazoo* without being identified. Jarlaxle wrote it himself. He enjoys rattling the nobility. | +2 |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:68: | 4th | **Three Nights** | A Xanathar's Guild prisoner appears — inexplicably — bound in the tavern's basement. Guard him for three nights while Jarlaxle makes arrangements. Xanathar sends escalating forces to retrieve him each night. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:69: | 5th | **The Compromised Eye** | Extract or protect Nar'l Xibrindas, the BD mole embedded in Xanathar's Guild. Jarlaxle forbids killing him — Nar'l is more valuable alive and in position. Three approaches: feed him a false report, extract him from the city, or confront Ahmaergo's counterintelligence source. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:77: Bregan D'aerthe teams favor misdirection over lethal force. Jarlaxle's preference is non-lethal resolution — confused enemies are more useful to him than dead ones.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:99: **Escalation:** Bregan D'aerthe moves to Suspicious if the party searches the house without BD renown; no tier change if they visit openly with Jarlaxle's introduction. If Fenerus has already been kidnapped before the party arrives, no escalation triggers — the kidnapping isn't traceable to the party from Bregan D'aerthe's perspective. **Founders' Day Clock: 1–3 days (longer if staking out for Krebbyg).**
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:105: Use the Autumn version of the theater (DH p. 75). Jarlaxle owns it under the false identity of Rongquan Mystere. One dressing room is kept empty as an emergency safe house for D'aerthe agents. Jarlaxle uses the theater for neutral-ground meetings with other factions.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:107: *Area P6 — Stage Manager's Office.* Among the theater paperwork: the *Letter Regarding Fenerus Stormcastle's House* from "K" to "J." Krebbyg warns Jarlaxle that strange eyes have been watching Fenerus's house and black snakes have been spotted over the Trades Ward; he recommends caution and possibly finding a replacement asset.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:113: **Escalation:** Bregan D'aerthe moves to Alert if the dressing room is searched without BD sanction. Renown penalties apply if the outpost is raided without an established relationship; if drow guards escape, Jarlaxle knows before the party reaches the door. **Founders' Day Clock: 2–4 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:120: | Suspicious | Nimblewright network harvests ambient intelligence; Fenerus drops messages at dead drops. Krebbyg reviews drops; Jarlaxle assesses whether the party is an asset or a problem. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:122: | Lockdown | Fel'rekt, Krebbyg, and Soluun deployed together. Jarlaxle intervenes personally, always with an offer rather than a fight. |
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:124: Jarlaxle reaches for leverage before force against the party. Direct confrontation only comes when the characters kill his lieutenants without first offering terms.

campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:11: If the party refused or turned Hostile, the campaign takes a harder shape. Imps summoned at the Asmodean Shrine maintain surveillance on the characters, feeding the Cassalanters continuous intelligence. Seffia's team joins the Eye heist at **Xanathar's Lair** as a competing faction. Willifort attempts to assassinate Jarlaxle, fails, then targets the characters in a follow-up attempt. This may push Jarlaxle to propose an alliance against the Cassalanters.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:47: **Escalation:** Cassalanters move to Alert (Tier 3). Imp survivors report to the family within hours; Seffia's team deploys within 2 days. If Bregan D'aerthe outposts have also been hit, the Cassalanters flag the party as a threat to their Faire surveillance arrangement. **Founders' Day Clock (Flamerule 1): 1–2 days.** The clock is the number of in-game days the operation takes out of the time left before Founders' Day; the Windmill's 3–5 days is counted the same way.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:95: - *Bregan D'aerthe's Report on the Grand Game* (Sea Maidens Faire)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:9: **All four villain factions run simultaneously.** No season villain. Xanathar's Guild, Manshoon's Zhentarim, the Cassalanters, and Bregan D'aerthe are active from session one, each pursuing a different angle on the same prize.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:48: The MacGuffin chain runs in a straight line: the Stone of Golorr names the vault but can't open it without all three Eyes. Xanathar holds Eye #1 (in Sylgar's fish bowl, unknowingly). The Cassalanters hold Eye #2. Jarlaxle holds Eye #3 aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Kolat Towers holds Manshoon, not an Eye. It's the fourth heist target; clearing it before the Vault pushes the party to Level 7.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:61: | [Player Factions Overview](player-factions-overview.md) | Before **Trollskull Alley** — renown rules, conflicting loyalties, Bregan D'aerthe unique mechanic |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:13: The City of Splendors is, canonically, a place where every power that matters watches every other power, waiting for an opening. When you pick a season villain, you convert that political texture into a genre piece: a crime drama where the Dragon Cult is running things this year. The other three factions become background noise. A campaign where Jarlaxle is the season villain isn't a Dragon Heist campaign; it's a Jarlaxle story. The city feels smaller than it should.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:77: ## Why the Nimblewright Thread Leads to Jarlaxle
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:79: Bregan D'aerthe recruits during **Trollskull Alley**, alongside every other player faction. Any PC can join. J.B. Nevercott's visit to Trollskull Manor happens in every campaign where the party hasn't severed contact, which means Jarlaxle is already operating in the city from Act I, through a persona that commits to nothing and explains nothing.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:81: What the nimblewright thread answers is who J.B. Nevercott is. The **Fireball!** investigation leads from the construct's origin to the Sea Maidens Faire, where Jarlaxle's carnival is the source. The moment the party connects the haberdasher to Zardoz Zord, and Zardoz Zord to the man behind the nimblewrights, is the reveal the campaign has been building toward. A party that joined Bregan D'aerthe in Act I has been working for a man whose full scope they didn't yet understand. That's the payoff.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:83: Jarlaxle's villain activity keys off the **Jarlaxle Informed** flag, set in **Fireball!** and read in **Gralhund Villa**. The flag records the party's contact with him, not his knowledge: he has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep. Faction membership doesn't escalate him; the flag does. A party that skips the nimblewright thread finds a Jarlaxle who stays in the background: useful, occasionally present, never dominant. The campaign has three complete villain arcs without him at the fore.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:55: **Jarlaxle** almost never reaches Lockdown without choosing to. His default response is leverage and observation. Direct confrontation comes only when the PCs have killed his lieutenants without first offering terms, and even then he opens with an offer.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:63: Jarlaxle's relationship with Xanathar is more managed. Nar'l Xibrindas, Xanathar's own senior advisor, reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell. The intelligence Nar'l provides has been selectively curated to keep the beholder occupied and predictable; Jarlaxle's goal is a manageable Xanathar, not a dead one. Xanathar suspects a mole in its organization and has been hunting in the wrong direction. It has not identified Nar'l.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:65: Jarlaxle and Manshoon maintain a fragile non-interference arrangement. Each considers the other the only genuine peer in the Grand Game, and neither wants direct conflict while other factions are more immediate problems. The arrangement is under strain: Manshoon has identified Fenerus Stormcastle as a Bregan D'aerthe cut-out and ordered his abduction. If that abduction proceeds, Jarlaxle will act unilaterally, and the non-interference collapses.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:67: The Cassalanters run a parallel track, largely independent of the others. They aren't competing with the other factions for the Stone; they intend to wait until someone activates it and reveals the vault's location, then move on the vault directly. Their outpost network is focused on gathering gold and ritual components. Cassalanter imps conduct ongoing surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire, but Jarlaxle's counter-intelligence hasn't detected it yet.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:86: **Bregan D'aerthe** reaches for leverage before force in almost every circumstance. What Jarlaxle wants is the political favor he has pursued for years: Lords' Alliance membership for Luskan. Direct confrontation comes only when the PCs have killed his lieutenants without offering terms. Jarlaxle holds debts and opportunities. A party that has crossed him simply moves to a different column in his ledger.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:88: He can also be reasoned with, and even joined. Membership opens during **Trollskull Alley**, before the party knows who Jarlaxle is, through the same surveillance-and-recruitment sequence that runs for every other faction. Joining changes the shape of **Sea Maidens Faire** completely: D'aerthe members find the Faire is a home base rather than a heist target, and Jarlaxle sends the party after another faction's Eye instead. Reporting the initial surveillance to the Watch closes membership permanently and leaves Jarlaxle a neutral third party in **Sea Maidens Faire**, never a potential ally. For parties who have not joined, two paths to a voluntary resolution exist before Tarsakh 20, when the ships depart. The alliance path asks for a credible political commitment to Luskan's Lords' Alliance bid in exchange for Eye #3; it requires a DC 16 Charisma (Persuasion) check, with advantage when the party brings concrete commitments from two or more faction contacts, and Jarlaxle detects empty promises. It bypasses the heist entirely, but strains every faction relationship built to that point. The patron path is available to D'aerthe members: at Renown 10 or above, Jarlaxle gives the Eye outright as a reward for proven service; at Initiate or Soldier rank (Renown 1–9), he runs the Zardoz Betrayal Pitch instead, a competence and loyalty test disguised as a job, and a party that reports back honestly sees him drop the disguise, reveal himself, and offer the Eye freely. Full mechanics for all three routes are in **Sea Maidens Faire** and the **Bregan D'aerthe** Factions guide page.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:99: - [Setting: Villains](../../setting/villains/) — personality phases and goals for Xanathar, Manshoon, the Cassalanters, and Jarlaxle
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:100: - [Bregan D'aerthe](../factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md) — response teams, revelation list
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:15: | Bregan D'aerthe | Exceptional intelligence; rare resources | Loyalty, and eventually a favor that reaches beyond Waterdeep |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:70: | **Warduke** | 25 | Once per quest, four Warrior Veterans from the Watch fight beside you in one operation on the surface. A sheet on the villain factions' response teams waits at the Portal's drop every third day. You can ask the Open Lord for one private audience per quest. Once per quest, a written request through your contact asks a Masked Lord, whom your contact will not name, to open a door, freeze an inquiry or apply political pressure. The Veterans fight as one fewer than the party, to a cap of four, and the six Alliance missions cannot use them. The sheet names a team only after the member has met it, and it says "the Splinter" and "the carnival fleet" until **Manshoon Named** and **Jarlaxle Unmasked** are marked. The Masked Lord is never named and is never Mirt, and Jalester does not know who it is. Laeral receives the member at the Palace the morning after the rank is named. A character who lies to her at an audience must beat a DC 16 Charisma (Deception) check. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:137: ### Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:165: ## Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:167: Bregan D'aerthe is the only villain faction available to player characters, and joining it changes the shape of a major quest. Faction lore and key members are on the **Bregan D'aerthe** organization page; quest hooks, renown and ranks, missions, response teams, and outposts are on the **Bregan D'aerthe** Factions guide page.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:174: - **Surveillance confronted:** The spies melt away and leave a single black eye patch on the cobblestones. J.B. Nevercott knocks the next morning.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:175: - **Party does nothing:** Surveillance continues through the tenday. J.B. Nevercott knocks regardless of whether the party noticed the watchers.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:177: **J.B. Nevercott** (haberdasher, burgundy coat, *hat of disguise*) arrives at Trollskull Manor and maintains the fiction until he chooses to drop it. The full First Meeting scene is in the **Bregan D'aerthe** Factions guide page. Set **BD Acknowledged** when it concludes.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:179: > [!warning]**Bregan D'aerthe Contact Severed**
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:180: > If the party reports the BD surveillance to the Watch, set **BD Contact Severed** and do not run the J.B. Nevercott scene. This flag closes BD membership for the entire campaign. Jarlaxle becomes aware of the party regardless; his intelligence network is thorough enough that the report changes nothing except the relationship. He operates as a neutral third party in **Sea Maidens Faire** rather than a potential ally. There is no second recruitment opportunity. The decision is permanent.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:182: Jarlaxle's true identity surfaces in **Fireball!** and **Sea Maidens Faire**, when the nimblewright thread leads the party to the carnival and its captain. Membership opens several quests before that disclosure.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:184: For parties who join, **Sea Maidens Faire** inverts: the Faire becomes a home base and Jarlaxle sends the party after another faction's Eye rather than holding the Faire as a heist target. The quest's inversion is the point — BD members experience the Grand Game from Jarlaxle's side of the table, where the resources and the objectives both belong to him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:186: Members take on Jarlaxle's long-term goal: Lords' Alliance recognition of Luskan's reformed governance. That obligation comes due in **Vault of Dragons**, where Jarlaxle proposes returning the gold publicly in the party's name in exchange for a letter of introduction to the Alliance council. It is what he has been working toward since he arrived in the city.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:190: The factions' interests are incompatible by design. The Lords' Alliance wants the gold returned to the city treasury intact. The Doom Raiders want Manshoon destroyed; his cell threatens their entire operation. Force Grey wants the Grand Game resolved before it spills into public view and embarrasses the city. Bregan D'aerthe wants a political favor that extends well beyond Waterdeep's walls.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:25: **Eye #3** — held by Jarlaxle Baenre, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. It began in Renaer Neverember's mourning locket. Manshoon's Zhentarim seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse, Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried off the Eye with the rest of the back office without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the same night. It reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout, and Manshoon's cell never held it. Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep. He is active from Act I through his personas — J.B. Nevercott in Trollskull Alley and Zardoz Zord at the Field of Triumph — and his villain escalation keys off the **Jarlaxle Informed** flag set during **Fireball!**, which records only that the party made contact with him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:30: > **Eye #3:** Jarlaxle Baenre — aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Jarlaxle knows exactly what it is. It passed from Renaer's locket to the Zhentarim, then to Xanathar's Guild in the warehouse raid, and Bregan D'aerthe agents inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it that night. Manshoon holds no Eye.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:41: | Bregan D'aerthe | Most complete picture via Nar'l Xibrindas; knows the Stone, the Eyes, and the vault; waiting to see which party proves most useful | Eye #3 | Stone + Eyes #1 and #2 |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:52: | **Bregan D'aerthe** | Normal operations | Nimblewright network harvests ambient intelligence; Fenerus Stormcastle drops messages at dead drops. Krebbyg Masq'il'yr reviews drops; Jarlaxle assesses whether the PCs are an asset or a problem | 1–2 drow gunslingers + drow escort; misdirection and non-lethal containment preferred | Fel'rekt Lafeen, Krebbyg, and Soluun Xibrindas deployed together. Jarlaxle intervenes personally, always with an offer rather than a fight |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:63: **Bregan D'aerthe.** Against the PCs, Jarlaxle reaches for leverage before force. Direct confrontation only comes when the PCs kill his lieutenants without first offering terms.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:73: | **Xanathar vs. Bregan D'aerthe** | Cautious rivalry — deep infiltration | Nar'l Xibrindas, Xanathar's own advisor, reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell — Bregan D'aerthe's best intelligence source on Guild movements. Xanathar suspects a mole; it hasn't identified Nar'l. Fel'rekt Lafeen observed the Gralhund Villa operation. Bregan D'aerthe purchases equipment through Grinda Garloth, a dependency neither faction has acknowledged. | *Correspondence with Nar'l Xibrindas* (Sewer Hideout), *A Recommendation for Materiel* (Fel'rekt Lafeen), *Report on the Seven Masks Theater* (Xanathar's Lair) |

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/01-overview.md:13: **Tarsakh 20 — Sea Maidens Faire departure.** Jarlaxle departs Waterdeep aboard the *Sea Maidens Faire* fleet at dawn on Tarsakh 20. After that date, the **Sea Maidens Faire** alliance path is no longer available and the faction relationship defaults to adversarial. This is the **Sea Maidens Faire** deadline only; it has no connection to **Cassalanter Villa**.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/02-operating-costs.md:85: - **Bregan D'aerthe (Jarlaxle):** Bregan D'aerthe PCs only — bankrolls full renovation up to 1,250 gp. Wants occasional use of the private upstairs dining room for discreet meetings (de facto courier dead drop). Introduces Watchful Order contact for *Glyphs of Warding* at 20% discount. No coin repayment; repayment is operational.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/08-notable-patrons.md:129: **Jarlaxle Baenre** *(Bregan D'aerthe, NE male drow; see the **Jarlaxle Baenre** page in Notable Figures and Act III)*
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/08-notable-patrons.md:133: **Running Jarlaxle at the bar:** He visits because the party interests him — they are his most unpredictable variable. Every visit should leave the party wondering what he wanted and whether they gave it to him. Play him as delighted, not menacing. The delight is worse. Drow PCs feel his attention differently — he has specific and complicated interest in drow operating independently on the surface.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:83: ## Bregan D'aerthe — Fel'rekt Lafeen (or Another Lieutenant)
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:85: *Jarlaxle's people are the only faction that might enjoy the tavern.*
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:87: **Who comes:** Fel'rekt Lafeen most often in the early campaign (Acts II–III), with 2–4 drow in excellent surface disguise. They order interesting things, tip well, cause no trouble. Jarlaxle himself appears separately in a different disguise each time (see Patron Profiles), preceded by Fel'rekt scoping the room first.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:90: - Party has interfered with Bregan D'aerthe's Eye operations or the Sea Maidens Faire
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:91: - Jarlaxle has decided the party is interesting enough to monitor closely
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:96: **If a BD member is in the party:** Fel'rekt does not disguise his heritage. Sits at the bar openly. Looks at the drow PC the way one professional looks at another who has found a good hiding spot. "Jarlaxle sends his regards, and wonders if you'd like to have dinner. Remalia's, Thursday. He'll know if you come." This scene never fires for a party with **BD Contact Severed**, and it never reopens membership: Bregan D'aerthe recruits only in **Trollskull Alley**.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:98: **What the party can do:** Meet Jarlaxle (full scene — see Patron Profiles), refuse him (he finds refusal interesting), or tail Fel'rekt back to the Sea Maidens Faire (DC 16 Dexterity Stealth vs. Fel'rekt's +6 Perception).
campaign/locations/gralhund-villa/g13-upstairs-foyer.md:9: If the BD assault sequence is active (*Jarlaxle Informed*): Jarlaxle's gunslingers fire through the G13 upper windows during the raid, targeting combatants in the foyer. The window glass has been blown inward. Cold air and the sounds of the city come through the gaps.
campaign/locations/gralhund-villa/g17-balcony.md:9: **BD assault route:** During the BD assault sequence (*Jarlaxle Informed*), Fel'rekt Lafeen and four drow use this balcony as their entry point — dropping from the rooftops and coming through the glass doors into G16. If the party is on the balcony when this happens, Fel'rekt acknowledges them with a glance and continues without breaking stride.
campaign/locations/gralhund-villa/g17-balcony.md:11: **Chase route:** If the nimblewright flees with the Stone, it drops from this balcony to the Coach House roofline and proceeds to the street. If Jarlaxle's team exits with the Stone, they withdraw the same way.

campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/03-eyecatcher-main-deck.md:21: The Eyecatcher's main deck is the Sea Maidens Faire primary landing zone for the harbor approach entry method. During the heist, the ship is in high alert — Jarlaxle has been tipped to the party's approach through arc-condition tracking. Two additional drow elite warriors are on deck (total: 4 at the aft castle). The nimblewright valets have been activated for defense; each has AC 13, 45 HP, Multiattack (sword × 2, +5 to hit, 1d6+3 slashing).
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:1: ﻿# J29/J30 — Eyecatcher: Jarlaxle's Cabins
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:5: **J29 — Guest Cabin:** The room where Zardoz Zord entertains visitors. Comfortable but not ostentatious — three chairs, a side table, maps of the Sword Coast displayed on the walls. Zord meets all guests here first. The persona is in full effect: cheerful, entrepreneurial, slightly flamboyant. A magic mouth trap on the door to J30 triggers if anyone other than Zord opens it (DC 16 Intelligence (Arcana) to detect), releasing a sound like a gong audible throughout the aft castle.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:7: **J30 — Captain's Main Cabin:** Jarlaxle's private space. The magic mouth trap, the jade chess set (2,500 gp, kept on a side table), a nimblewright guard standing in the corner, and a trapdoor in the floor — arcane locked (Jarlaxle's key or DC 20 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools)) — opening to the Scarlet Marpenoth's pressurized hatch collar (U1). The complete ledger is kept here in a locked drawer (DC 16 Thieves' Tools for the drawer; DC 18 Investigation to find within the desk's papers).
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:13: If the party asks about the nimblewright: Zord excuses himself briefly, steps through the wardrobe into J30 (he closes the door behind him), and returns with the Gralhund name, address, and the note that the unit had "combat programming — unusual for a household model." He presents this as a simple ledger lookup. Consequence: Jarlaxle has full intelligence on the party's investigation. He deploys BD agents to Gralhund Villa.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:15: **Bregan D'Aerthe Member:** A drow party member (or any party member with BD affiliation established) is escorted not to J29 but through the wardrobe directly to J30. The hat comes off. Jarlaxle sits without the persona. He offers the complete ledger as a gift (no check required) and asks only to be in the room when the Stone conversation happens. Renown: +1 for sharing intelligence with BD, +2 for withholding.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:19: **Trapdoor to Scarlet Marpenoth:** Arcane locked (DC 20 Thieves' Tools or Jarlaxle's key). A party that bypasses it finds U1 below — the pressurized entry hatch to the submersible.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:25: During Sea Maidens Faire, J29 is Jarlaxle's command post — he manages the heist's moving parts from here, not from J30 or J31 (training area). The Zardoz Zord persona may or may not be in effect depending on the party's prior relationship with him (see Sea Maidens Faire: Jarlaxle's Disposition table per BD relationship state).
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:27: The complete ledger is no longer here — it was secured to the Scarlet Marpenoth (U4, Jarlaxle's stateroom) after Fireball. The jade chess set remains. The nimblewright guard is active at combat readiness.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/04-eyecatcher-zords-office.md:29: The trapdoor to the Scarlet Marpenoth is unlocked during the heist if Jarlaxle expects to need a fast extraction route.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/05-scarlet-marpenoth.md:19: The control room occupies two levels: U7a (observation deck, upper) and U7b (command center, lower). U7a contains Jarlaxle's fallback position chair, a bronze periscope, and a bronze crystal ball on a teak stand. U7b contains the pilot console, three gnome pilot stations, and two drow elite warrior guards.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/05-scarlet-marpenoth.md:34: **Accessing U7:** From U1, the path runs through U2 (engine room, Breena Bafflestone on watch), through the stateroom corridor (U3–U6), and up the ladder to U7a. Breena is cooperative unless the party is clearly unauthorized; DC 13 Persuasion to explain their presence convincingly (she's a pragmatic gnome who doesn't enjoy confrontation). The drow elite warriors in U7b will not allow access to U7a without Jarlaxle's authorization; DC 16 Deception to claim authorization.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/05-scarlet-marpenoth.md:36: **What Jarlaxle does if the crystal ball is accessed:** He discovers within 24 hours that the ball was used. He cannot identify who. He does not deploy to Gralhund Villa — this party does not need his help and he is curious rather than angry. He files it as information.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/05-scarlet-marpenoth.md:42: **U4 — Jarlaxle's Stateroom:** Locked (DC 18 Thieves' Tools). Harp, harpsichord, and a magic one-way window showing the sea outside. The trunk in the corner releases a mechanical spider swarm on unauthorized opening (DC 16 Perception to notice the trap; DC 14 Dexterity to disable; swarm: AC 12, 44 HP, Multiattack). The trunk's false bottom contains the complete ledger after Fireball (if the Eyecatcher ledger was stolen, Jarlaxle moved this copy here; if not stolen, the J30 copy remains there).
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/05-scarlet-marpenoth.md:48: During Sea Maidens Faire, the Scarlet Marpenoth is Jarlaxle's designated extraction route and Sea Maidens Faire's highest-value intelligence target (it holds the *Stone of Golorr* in U4 if Jarlaxle has acquired it through quest conditions). Jarlaxle keeps Eye #3 in U4 in either case; it reached him the night before Ches 1. The pressurized keel collar is unlocked from Jarlaxle's command (he expects to need it). The three drow gunslingers are at combat readiness in their staterooms.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/05-scarlet-marpenoth.md:50: The gnome pilots in U7b are under orders to submerge at Jarlaxle's signal — a two-tone chime from U7a. Submerging takes 5 minutes; the exterior hatch seals at 3 minutes. Any creature outside the hull when the hatch seals is left behind.
campaign/locations/sea-maidens-faire/area-overview.md:11: > - **Scarlet Marpenoth**: Lantanese submersible, keel-docked beneath the Eyecatcher via a pressurized collar. Not visible from the surface except at night with a DC 18 Perception check from the water's edge. It has been moored in the harbor since Jarlaxle reached Waterdeep in Marpenoth 1491 and keel-docks beneath the Eyecatcher when the fleet arrives on Ches 21. Jarlaxle keeps Eye #3 in his stateroom (U4).

campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:11: ***Bregan D'Aerthe Observer.*** The drow woman at the Yawning Portal balcony table is the campaign's earliest Bregan D'Aerthe presence. She has no name in ev-01, and they're not meant to notice her unless they ask who is watching the well. She reappears in Trollskull Alley as a background presence, same table on different evenings. Players who look into her background will find she's part of Jarlaxle's Waterdeep intelligence network monitoring the Undermountain entrance. By the time they reach the Sea Maidens Faire in Sea Maidens Faire, they can look back at ev-01 and understand that Jarlaxle has had eyes on them since their first night in the city.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:75: ### Jarlaxle Watching
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:77: Zardoz Zord watches every bout from the Faire's reserved box, expansive and theatrically delighted. A **DC 18 Wisdom (Insight)** check reads the delight as genuine but not uncontrolled, something considerably more precise underneath the showmanship, the focused patience of a man evaluating something carefully. He does not initiate contact beyond post-tournament congratulations. He is noting the party for Sea Maidens Faire.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:79: Cross-ref the **Jarlaxle Baenre** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:111: Zardoz Zord invited the party to fight in his sponsored gladiatorial slots at the Field of Triumph. Four rounds of nonlethal combat. The final round put them across the sand from Meloon Wardragon, who shook their hands afterward and asked their names. Xanathar's Guild was watching.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:119: The party competed in Zardoz Zord's sponsored gladiatorial slots at the Field of Triumph, met Meloon Wardragon in the final round, and received an unsolicited contact from Xanathar's Guild. The prize purses helped close the renovation gap. The tournament is done; the Grand Opening is next.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-07-the-twin-parades.md:26: The party is participating in Waterdeep civic life now — they have neighbors in this crowd, faction contacts, and a tavern that people here have already visited. The Twin Parades fall on Ches 21, the opening day of Fleetswake (Ches 21–30), the maritime festival that fills Waterdeep Harbor with tall ships. The **Sea Maidens Faire carnival fleet sails into harbor this morning**. From the parade route's eastern end, the party may notice the carnival flotilla's banners in the harbor. The *Scarlet Marpenoth* is not part of the arrival: Jarlaxle's submarine has been moored in the harbor all year, since he reached Waterdeep in Marpenoth 1491. **Thread: Sea Maidens Faire reconnaissance window begins.** The Fleetswake period (Ches 21–30) is when Jarlaxle's ships are in harbor and accessible.

Vessin is positioned along the parade route. Full mission write-up in the **Bregan D'aerthe** Faction Events.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/overview.md:64: A carnival captain named Zardoz Zord invited them to fight in his sponsored gladiatorial slots at the Field of Triumph. They met Meloon Wardragon in the final round, who shook their hands afterward and asked their names. The Grand Opening filled the house — and Ammalia Cassalanter stayed two hours, learned everyone's name, and left with the social grace that makes a room feel larger after she's gone.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:43: ***Frustration That Becomes Coherent.*** The Faire visit feels like a stall because it is one — the Gralhund nimblewright is not there. This is intentional. Following a real lead to a productive-but-incomplete result is exactly what good investigative fiction builds tolerance for. The payoff arrives the moment the party moves north and the detector changes behavior on Saerdoun Street. The intelligence gained at the Faire (the ledger, Jarlaxle's identity, the Cassalanter entry, the BD private meeting) makes the detour retroactively coherent.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:45: ***Jarlaxle's Conditional Activation.*** If the party reaches the Faire, Jarlaxle enters the Grand Game as an active participant and may pre-position BD agents at Gralhund Villa. If the party bypasses the Faire entirely, he remains a background presence — aware, watching, not deployed. This is not a punishment for skipping Scene 4. It is a realistic consequence of investigative depth. The party that turns over more stones gives Jarlaxle more surface to react to.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/overview.md:38: - **Captain Zardoz Zord / Jarlaxle Baenre** (Sea Maidens Faire) — Bregan D'Aerthe master; knows about the fireball, the gnome, the Stone, the Eyes and the vault before the party arrives (he has known of the last three since reaching Waterdeep); decides how much to share and at what price
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/overview.md:59: The House of Inspired Hands held a nimblewright of its own, built three months before the blast, and the detector device never used to find the runaway that fled a month later. Nine sweeps across four wards cleared every legitimate owner and left two signals standing: the harbor, where the Sea Maidens Faire's three ships lit the detector like a chandelier, and the North Ward, where a single reading on Saerdoun Street spun the device harder than anything else in the city. The Faire turned out to be a detour with an intelligence yield — the nimblewright's seller, its original buyer, a ledger that named the Gralhunds, and Jarlaxle Baenre behind the Zord persona. The detector's final reading pointed to a gated estate in the North Ward.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:9: What breaks if the party doesn't get the Stone? The campaign continues — but every subsequent negotiation runs from weakness rather than strength. Jarlaxle deals with petitioners differently than he deals with principals. Whichever faction holds the Stone begins locating the vault within days. The party is racing an active countdown rather than holding a controlling position. This is consequential.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:17: The optional daytime escalation elements exist because the nighttime state provides natural attrition from rival factions: Zhents and guards fight each other, Jarlaxle's drow thin the opposition, and the party navigates through the fighting rather than generating all of it. The daytime state has none of that. If a fully-equipped level 4 party enters during the day against the standard roster, the encounter may feel thin. The optional additions correct for this without changing the fundamental dynamic.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:23: This quest follows that philosophy. The ten-beat raid timeline is a spine, not a script. The GM can drop the party into it at any beat, and party action can derail it entirely. Floxin does not inevitably succeed at anything. Jarlaxle's team arriving early changes the calculus. The party drawing the Barracks reinforcements away from Beat 9 may let Floxin hold the ground floor.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:27: ## Jarlaxle's Test
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:29: The intelligence Jarlaxle offers in ev-01 is accurate. He has the ledger. He has the upper floor layout. He knows where Xanathar's gazers are. He gives the party all of it, freely, without apparent agenda.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:33: Parties that find the BD team during ev-02 reconnaissance and raise it with Jarlaxle get a smile and a redirect. Parties that find it and don't raise it get a warmer reception next time. Parties that never find it get a practical demonstration that faction intelligence is always partial. This connects directly to the **Fireball!** design note on investigation quality having consequences: the relationship with Jarlaxle is indexed to how the party handles exactly these moments.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:45: If the chase fails and the Stone reaches a faction safe house, the campaign continues from a weaker position. Jarlaxle will deal with the party. Xanathar will not. Manshoon's cell now knows the party is active and failed. These consequences reshape the social landscape of subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is **Faction Outposts**' problem, not this quest's.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:57: Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's North Ward operations become more careful. Jarlaxle leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown (who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn) define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:1: # Design Notes: Bregan D'aerthe First Meeting
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:5: The restored draft marked **Bregan D'aerthe Joined** and **BD Contact Severed** as party-wide flags, so one report to the Watch closed Bregan D'aerthe to everyone for the whole campaign. The event now follows the Harpers and Doom Raiders models. Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate and answers for themselves, and both outcomes are marked with the character's name.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:7: The Watch branch is the hard case. A character who gives the sergeant their name is severed for good, which is the rule the Factions guide and **Trollskull Alley** both state. The watchers still vanish at dusk for the whole party. Nevercott keeps his word to "be in touch regardless" and calls on Ches 13 for any candidate who did not give a name. The source says contact ends "for the time being", and the guide says "permanently". The event follows the guide for the severed character and the source for everyone else, so one player's report cannot remove another player's story.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:9: An attack on Nevercott also marks **BD Contact Severed**. The source gives him no combat or escape behavior. The restored draft invented a glass bead and an obscuring cloud that kept him away for three tendays, and both were cut. He now walks out through the crowd, and the attacker is closed out like a reporter.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:21: Appendix B has Nevercott keep the fiction until a drow character steps aside. That fails for a party without a drow, so the event has him wait a quarter of an hour for a drow candidate and then ask the first candidate to show him the courtyard. Every candidate gets the scene separately. The offer and its key phrases come from the source: the eye patch calling card, the name of Bregan D'aerthe, the black card with a silver ship, "declining costs you nothing" and "I'll be in touch shortly regardless". He does not brief the first assignment here. He delivers **The Handkerchief and the Girl** in person at Trollskull Manor at dusk on Ches 20, the eve of the Twin Parades, which replaces the draft's unsigned theater tickets.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:23: Nevercott has no voice profile in `character-voices`, so he is voiced from Appendix B: a courteous, amused Sea Ward haberdasher with long, well-balanced sentences, no swearing and one precise question for every three he answers. He never names Jarlaxle and never confirms anything, and a player's guess meets polite incomprehension. A profile for him belongs in the skill if the user wants one.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:31: The renovation financing that **The Factions Come Calling** attaches to Bregan D'aerthe (up to 1,250 gp, repayable in "operational access") is not carried here, since no source text decides how Nevercott would offer it. Quilm, the drow bouncer candidate, has no role in the event.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:1: # Bregan D'aerthe First Meeting
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:5: > This Social Event occurs during **The Factions Come Calling**. It begins on Ches 3, when Bregan D'aerthe's watchers start following the party, and ends when J.B. Nevercott leaves Trollskull Manor after his visit. In this Event, the characters can:
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:10: > - Receive J.B. Nevercott, a haberdasher, who names Bregan D'aerthe and offers a first assignment to a candidate who steps aside.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:16: > Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate, whatever their ancestry, and each candidate answers for themselves. The watchers stay closest to drow characters. Nevercott makes no offer to characters who belong to another faction. They and any companions can stay for the hat patter but gain nothing from the visit.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:18: > If no character qualifies, Nevercott talks about hats for a quarter of an hour, sells nothing and leaves, and the Event ends with no membership outcome.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:22: > J.B. Nevercott is Jarlaxle Baenre in a *hat of disguise*, so nobody in the room can describe his face afterward. He never admits to being anyone else, even if a player guesses, and nobody in this Event says Jarlaxle's name aloud. **Jarlaxle Unmasked** is marked later, for each member separately. Until then, Bregan D'aerthe speakers say "the captain" or "J."
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:24: > Jarlaxle is testing the party to see who notices the watchers and what they do about it. A report to the Watch counts as an answer.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:26: > The watchers are Jarlaxle's three lieutenants. Fel'rekt Lafeen and Krebbyg Masq'il'yr shadow the party from dusk until dawn, and Soluun Xibrindas takes the day from shade and doorways. After dark Soluun also hunts elf and half-elf sailors in the Dock Ward on his own account, the subject of the Doom Raiders mission **The Dockside Killer**. Jarlaxle knows and has not decided what to do. Nothing in this Event lets the party connect the two, and Soluun never speaks to anyone.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:28: > Ryvarra has watched the well from the Yawning Portal's balcony for three months and sends Jarlaxle a report every tenday. Her latest report names the party, and she tells the lieutenants where the party goes.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:33: > - **If the party confronts a watcher:** Nevercott knocks at 10 a.m. the next morning.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:34: > - **If the party does nothing:** Nevercott knocks at 10 a.m. on Ches 10.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:35: > - **If a candidate reports the watchers to the Watch:** the watchers are gone by dusk the same day, and the Watch's letter arrives the next morning. Nevercott still knocks at 10 a.m. on Ches 13 for every candidate who did not report.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:36: > - **If several branches occur:** the earliest date applies, and Nevercott speaks only to candidates who have not reported.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:83: > Ryvarra (Neutral, Drow, she/her) :: Jarlaxle's field observer at the Yawning Portal, who plays a Calimshan cloth merchant in the middle of a long negotiation.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:93: > She will not discuss where she lodges, whom she writes to, or anything about Bregan D'aerthe.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:137: A party that notices the watchers and does nothing runs the same branch as one that never notices them. The glimpses repeat each day and night until Nevercott's knock on Ches 10. A character who tries to follow a watcher loses them within three streets, because the watchers split up at the first corner and know the crowds better than any visitor. Proceed to **The Haberdasher at the Door**.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:159: The eye patch is plain, worn soft and unmarked, and examining it reveals nothing else. If the confrontation is by day and the party includes an elf or half-elf, the hood turns toward that character and stays a moment longer than it does for the others. This is Soluun's habit and has no effect here, but **The Dockside Killer** builds on it. J.B. Nevercott knocks at the front door of Trollskull Manor at 10 a.m. the next morning. Proceed to **The Haberdasher at the Door**.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:183: > He knows nothing about Bregan D'aerthe, and he will not ask.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:195: > Mark **BD Contact Severed** for each character who gives Dunmere their name. A character who stands beside the reporter and gives no name is not marked, and neither is any companion. The watchers are gone from Trollskull Alley by dusk, for the whole party. Jarlaxle has the report from his Watch contacts before the ink is dry.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:197: > Jarlaxle closes the file on each reporting character. He treats them as a civilian variable in **Sea Maidens Faire**, and nobody from Bregan D'aerthe raises membership with them again. Ryvarra stays at the Portal and keeps filing her reports, because nothing she does is against the law.

He knocks at 10 a.m. on Ches 13, and he does not mention the Watch unless a candidate raises it. If a reporting character speaks to him, read or paraphrase the following:

I heard that the new proprietors of Trollskull Manor were people of taste, and I hoped to introduce myself before you went elsewhere."
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:233: > J.B. Nevercott (Chaotic Neutral, Drow in a hat of disguise, he/him) :: a courteous and amused Sea Ward haberdasher who enjoys the visit more than he shows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:235: > Nevercott is Friendly and in no hurry. He speaks in long, well-turned sentences, compliments the party's taste and shares his opinions about hat brims for as long as anyone will listen. He asks one precise question for every three he answers. He never confirms anything about himself beyond what is printed on the card, and he never swears or raises his voice. If a character presses him about Bregan D'aerthe before a candidate has stepped aside, he answers with something about hats.

He looks at the characters' hands and weapons more often than at their heads, and he has not once glanced at the hats he says he sells.

Companions and characters who joined another faction are not enrolled. Leave it unmarked if every candidate declines or no candidate is eligible.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:464: > - **BD Contact Severed** — mark for each character who gives Sergeant Dunmere their name in a report to the Watch, and for any character who attacks Nevercott. campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:485: We noticed figures watching Trollskull Manor from doorways and rooftops, and a haberdasher called J.B. Nevercott arrived with a hat box and a great many opinions about brims. When he got us alone he told us he represented a mercenary company called Bregan D'aerthe and offered us a first assignment, which he said would cost us nothing to refuse. Each of us gave our own answer, and we were told to expect him again at dusk on the eve of the Twin Parades.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:489: We told Sergeant Dunmere about the watchers, and by dusk they were gone from Trollskull Alley. A letter came the next morning saying the Watch had found nothing, and we did not hear from Bregan D'aerthe again.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/design-notes.md:15: Nevercott's single instruction exists to be tested. Nobody can see the party break it, and the party holds the cloth for an hour. The debrief asks the question directly. An honest answer earns a bonus, and a lie costs every bonus if Vessin's book contradicts it. Vessin writes down what the party says in front of her, so the party learns that the company keeps records even on children in crates. That fits the faction's long game, in which the company asks for competence and discretion and rewards honesty it did not ask for.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/design-notes.md:27: Vessin's age, the Faire performer who left the cloth and the flower-stall detail are invented here. The source does not state Bartlethorpe's alignment or species, so this page treats him as a Lawful Neutral human. Contradictions with files outside this folder are left unedited and listed for the Bregan D'aerthe section of the out-of-scope notes:

Neither agent appears in this mission, and the party never learns who they are.

Nobody searches a clerk who has stood on the same spot at every parade for twenty years, so he makes a good dead drop. The agent who placed it cannot be seen near Vessin, so Nevercott hires strangers to carry it.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/overview.md:17: On the evening of Ches 20, J.B. Nevercott calls at Trollskull Manor with a hat box under his arm and gives the members two small white cards. One names a clerk who will stand in the shadow of the Great Drunkard during tomorrow's parade, and the other names a girl who lives in a crate near the wharves.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/overview.md:21: Bregan D'aerthe uses couriers nobody would think to follow. One of them is Vessin, a tiefling girl of about eleven who lives in a crate at the corner of Net Street and Dock Street and keeps a book of everything she sees. This morning a Faire performer pinned a perfumed silk handkerchief into the breast pocket of Maester Roderick Bartlethorpe at the Fleetswake flower stalls. The cloth carries a message in a scent language that only the company's couriers can read, and the message arranges a meeting between two of the company's agents.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/overview.md:23: Bartlethorpe is a Castle Ward clerk who has stood in the same place for every public event for twenty years, which makes him a dependable dead drop. The performer cannot be seen near Vessin, so Nevercott, who is Jarlaxle Baenre in a *hat of disguise*, sends new members to collect the cloth. He also wants to see whether they can do a small, odd job without making a scene, and whether they open a thing they were told to leave alone.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/overview.md:27: Nevercott gives the members the job at dusk on Ches 20, standing, with his hat on and the hat box under his arm. He sends the companions out of the room first. He then tells the members who to find, where the man will be, where to take the cloth and when, and his only instruction is that the handkerchief is not to be opened.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/overview.md:47: Each participating Bregan D'aerthe member gains 2 base Renown for delivering the handkerchief to Vessin. Members earn +1 each for taking it without raising an alarm, for putting it in Vessin's hands by 14:00, and for reading the scent code and saying so to Nevercott.

Vessin has already told him what the party said in front of her. The pouch of coins described in **Coin Pouches** reaches the manor two days later. **The Wazoo Affair** becomes available when an individual member reaches Renown 3 and 3rd level.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/overview.md:57: - **Vessin** (Bregan D'aerthe): a tiefling courier of about eleven who receives the cloth and records the party in her book.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:5: The exposé comes from the source: a lurid piece on devil worship and orgies among unnamed Waterdhavian noble families, written by Jarlaxle and delivered by break-in. The restored draft made it accurate. It described a ritual space matching the Cassalanter villa's lower temple and credited a witness account to Nar'l Xibrindas, so Xanathar's Guild would have known the Cassalanters were infernalists before the party did. That contradicts the standing rule that no other faction knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:7: The user ruled that Jarlaxle knows about the Cassalanters through a spy and won't tell members who haven't worked it out. The spy is Vessa, the Bregan D'aerthe doppelganger embedded in Cassalanter society, who reads thoughts and learned of the Asmodean pact and the children's contract that way. Here the piece stays rumor-shaped. It names no family and no witness. Its only particulars are an Aveen Street cellar and a Southern Ward windmill, given as footmen would give them, and it scatters hooks that fit several great houses: charity, a pleasant manner, a large fortune. Its purpose is pressure. Jarlaxle wants the Cassalanters to feel watched, and wants to see how they react and whom they send, without anyone tracing the piece to what he knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:19: The source's desk note puts Gaxly's Black Viper source at "anonymous, Tethyrian, well-dressed". That matches the household secretary Corisande Brisenne, who planted the story that **The Black Viper Investigation** has the Order of the Gauntlet unpick. Nevercott has no reason to know this, and the note is a loose thread here and a clue there. **Black Viper Source Noted** is read by that mission as Advantage on the first layer.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:21: ## Nevercott
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:23: J.B. Nevercott is Jarlaxle in a haberdasher's coat. He briefs at the Yawning Portal because the source and the organization page both place him there, and he says "Do not read it" because the source says he expects them to. The closing question, "Did you find it informative?", is the source's too, and it is where a member's honest guess about the piece earns a bonus. He never confirms a guess, because that would show what he knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:25: Nevercott is Jarlaxle in disguise, so his speech follows the Jarlaxle profile in `character-voices` and the haberdasher persona in the First Meeting. He is courteous and unhurried, he answers with longer sentences than the question needs, and he doesn't swear. No BD speaker says "Jarlaxle" here, in line with the **Jarlaxle Unmasked** gate.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:33: Invented names, all for minor NPCs: Pennet (the candlemaker's apprentice), Hovan Dree (the third-floor scrivener) and the false firm Harrow & Pell. Pennet's and Hovan's ages and trades are invented to fill the source's "other tenants". Contradictions outside this folder, listed for the Bregan D'aerthe section of the out-of-scope notes:
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:36: - `campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md` line 51 says Jarlaxle "knows about their infernal bargain through intelligence". Under the user's ruling this is now correct, and the page could name Vessa as the source.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/overview.md:21: Jarlaxle Baenre wrote the exposé himself, in the voice of a gossip sheet. It describes devil worship and orgies among unnamed noble families of Waterdeep, with secret bargains over the wine and enough rumor about shrines and windmills to make a reader wonder. It names no family and offers no proof. Jarlaxle knows the truth about the Cassalanters because his doppelganger agent Vessa has lived among them for months and read it in their thoughts. He won't tell the members, and the piece can't be traced to what he knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/overview.md:25: Jarlaxle sends the document under his haberdasher's name, J.B. Nevercott. He tells the party not to read it, expects them to anyway, and wants to hear what they make of it.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/overview.md:33: The document takes about twenty minutes to read, and Nevercott expects his instruction not to open it to be ignored. The piece names no family, quotes no witness, and gives each detail as something servants and tradesmen said, so it reads as scandal rather than evidence. A careful reader notices that nearly any great house could find itself in it.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/overview.md:45: The Wazoo prints the piece two days after Gaxly finds it. The party can watch the men arrive, follow their carriage and talk to Gaxly afterwards. Nothing proves who sent them, but the false firm and the covered crest show that someone with money and a reason for care read the piece.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/design-notes.md:5: The restored draft made Ott a halfling in a fish-stained apron and had Krebbyg collect him on the fourth morning, which solved a mystery the source leaves open. In Dragon Heist, Ott appears in the cellar in iron bands nobody can explain and vanishes after the third attack, and the Bregan D'aerthe appendix adds that nothing arrives to say why. This version keeps both. Ott is a dwarf in an eyestalk skullcap, the bands go when the third night ends, and nobody in Bregan D'aerthe, Ott included, knows who put him there.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/design-notes.md:7: Leaving the cause open means the mission needs another reason for the party to care, and the captain's note supplies it. Jarlaxle did not set the trap, but he saw its use. Xanathar cannot do without the one man who feeds Sylgar, so the three nights show what the Guild will spend to get him back, and Fel'rekt counts it from the roof of the Bent Nail. The party never learns Fel'rekt is there unless it looks, and he never comes down. Krebbyg is a poor source and says so, which keeps the captain's real interest out of reach until later events.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/design-notes.md:7: The three-tier order is the mission's tension. Nevercott asks for the gentlest outcome first and the cruelest last, which is what the sources imply. The earlier draft's "he stays alive and in place" order removed that. The four drow at the stair carry the cruel order as a second pair of hands. A spymaster who writes "swiftly and without pain" and then arms four people against the answer is a more interesting patron than one who writes a single clean instruction.
