campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:5: The restored draft said Brimel Crestfall "agreed" to the handoff for reasons he had not articulated, and the readaloud said he was "paid." The two cannot both stand, and a man who sells his employers' secrets from conscience would have to know what the secret was. Brimel does not, so he is paid. The household's wine merchant, who has been on Bregan D'aerthe's books for a year, offered him 300 gp for a floor plan. He took 150 gp a tenday ago, and the other 150 gp is the envelope the member carries to the doorway. He wants to leave Waterdeep, and the berth aboard the *Heartbreaker* is the thing he cannot ask for.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:7: His fear comes from **The Wazoo Affair**. The Cassalanters sent solicitors to a gossip sheet and then began questioning their own staff, so a steward who has carried baskets to the family's windmill reasonably believes he will be blamed. That link is the only one this mission needs from M2, and it holds with **Wazoo Exposé Published** and **Cassalanter Reaction Observed** as backstory. Nobody the party meets, except Bregan D'aerthe, needs to know about infernalism. Brimel knows about private gatherings, a hired cab with the curtains drawn, and Lord Victoro's word "resolution," which he takes for a debt. The restored line about Madam Ammalia visiting the week after her youngest was born is cut, because it points at the children's pact and the twins are eight.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:11: The restored draft had Jarlaxle fishing for a secret the Wazoo piece had not yet turned up. Under the user's ruling, "Jarlaxle knows about the Cassalanters, he has a spy there. He won't tell the members unless they figure it out themselves," he already knows, through Vessa, the doppelganger embedded in the Cassalanter social circle. She reads thoughts, which is how he learned of the Asmodean pact and the children's contract. Vessa's cover reaches banquet halls, not a windmill on Coachlamp Lane. Brimel's plan fills that gap, and Krebbyg's delight at the Brandath map is real, because it is the company's first sight of where the family goes.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:13: The speakers never confirm any of it to a member. The exception is the **Figuring It Out** rule: a member who says it in their own words, or shows proof, hears Zardoz or Nevercott admit the company has known for some time. A hunch does not unlock it, and the speaker does not name Vessa. **Cassalanter Pact Shared with BD** records the admission for **Cassalanter Villa** (unconverted), where Vessa helps that member openly, and for **Officer**, whose assessment may then state the truth.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:17: Zardoz Zord never lets a silence go unfilled, and his voice profile says so. In **The Theater's Back Room** he sits in the owner's box for the whole showcase and says nothing. The break is deliberate, and it is the loudest thing he does in the mission.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:42: If **Faction Outposts** has already raided the windmill, a handoff is redundant, so the mission becomes a debrief. Bregan D'aerthe has run the exchange itself, and Fel'rekt took the plan off Brimel at the showcase. The company asks the member for what a floor plan cannot give, a firsthand account. The plan confirms what the party saw. The member walks out with the key because the dressing room is where Zardoz and Krebbyg want the talk, and **Seven Masks Back Room** is set there.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:48: **Seven Masks Back Room** is the dressing-room safe house from **Faction Outposts**, which guide 08 describes as Jarlaxle's emergency safe house for agents. The Initiate benefit lists a safe house aboard the *Heartbreaker* or *Hellraiser*, not in the theater, and the Renown 3 door briefing admits a member only to the lobby and booking office. The dressing room was never open to members until this mission. A member who raided it during **Faction Outposts** gets forgiveness instead, and the Alert tier the raid set drops to Suspicious. **The Dive** and **Houseless Noble** read the outcome, because Krebbyg briefs there and the Renown 50 ceremony needs a place that is not the stage door. **Officer** and **Commander** do not read it and meet at the stage door, the booking office or aboard ship.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:7: ***The Guild is after an asset, not a prize.*** Ahmaergo treats Bregan D'aerthe as the Guild's enemy and a submarine as the one thing the captain cannot replace. Nothing in this mission can be picked up and carried off, so the stakes stay on the boat and the people around it.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:11: ***The Faire left on Tarsakh 20.*** The draft had the *Eyecatcher* anchored 200 yards from a pier, and Krebbyg saying the Faire was committed to Waterdeep for another season. Neither fits the calendar, which closes the Faire's paths on Tarsakh 20 and has the ships leave on schedule. The event now has the first mate take the *Eyecatcher* while the captain stays ashore with Krebbyg, Fel'rekt and a small team. The arc-h structure doc puts a drow mage, not the captain, at the *Eyecatcher*'s helm, so Zardoz Zord can stay in Waterdeep without contradiction. The submarine goes to a reserve berth under the outer end of the old Faire pier at Smugglers' Dock, 20 feet down. Soluun knew that berth because he slept aboard, so a sale made before the Faire sailed fits the mooring the Guild found.

Krebbyg and Fel'rekt do not fight here. Where they do, in **Commander** and **Houseless Noble**, they use the *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist* Drow Gunslinger. The draft made Krebbyg a calm handler who "does not dive" for reasons nobody explained. He is now rash, frightened and talkative, says "darling" and "Ask Fel", and doesn't dive because he would drown loudly. Fel'rekt arrives, counts options on his fingers and says there is another way. Zardoz is loud and generous and never mentions drow, the Underdark or Luskan. campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r10-officer/design-notes.md:14: The draft's folio duplicated the ledger that **Fireball!** already hands over, since Jarlaxle gives the complete ledger to Bregan D'aerthe members in the cabin and the party can steal it. The records stay as the Officer benefit because the Factions Guide names them. A party that already holds the ledger gets buyer-trace annotations instead. These are invented: where each unit is kept in its buyer's house, and when it last left. They give Advantage on one check and add no new facts about the buyers. The handover pays no Renown, so the +2 for taking the records from the Faire stays a separate reward for a member who steals them.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/design-notes.md:9: A member who has marked **Jarlaxle Unmasked** gets Jarlaxle himself, and every other member gets Zardoz Zord, so each key beat has a paired readaloud. The coin's spider is the rejected goddess in both versions. Zardoz mocks "the old eight-legged lady with a knife in her", and Jarlaxle calls the coin a joke at her expense.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/ev-01-houseless-noble.md:525: The leader of Bregan D'aerthe calls a member aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* to name them a Houseless Noble of the company's inner circle and to offer a favor in exchange for the full run of its ship and its network.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s01-coin-pouches/design-notes.md:5: The restored draft was a delivery with nothing to decide. The source gives only "small, unmarked black pouches of coins from an anonymous source", and the amounts, the note and the anchor were remix additions. The anchor is gone with the old handkerchief, because the Twin Parades version of **The Handkerchief and the Girl** has no knot code to decode. In its place the member gets a three-lead trace that fails on purpose. Each lead ends at someone who was paid ahead for one small job. That shows how Bregan D'aerthe keeps its drow out of sight, since nobody ever meets two links of the chain.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s01-coin-pouches/design-notes.md:7: The Dunstan Rook scene uses the source's third support, buying off or removing people who threaten the party. No other Bregan D'aerthe event used it. Dunstan is a human bouncer on Emmek Frewn's payroll, so he can threaten the member within the current Act I state without touching **The Shard Shunners** or Kelso Fiddlewick. Fel'rekt buys him off with 40 gp and a coach seat because he refuses to kill when another option exists. The contrast with the pouch chain is deliberate. The money trail shows Nevercott's discipline, and the Dunstan trail shows Kreb and Fel'rekt getting it done in an evening.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s01-coin-pouches/design-notes.md:11: The pouch goes to one named member by a fixed rule, because membership is individual and the gold should have an owner. The note, *A more interesting assignment follows shortly*, lives only in this event, and **Three Nights** should refer to the pouch without repeating the text. The 100 gp slip is cut from the same black stock as Nevercott's First Meeting card, which replaces the old "no watermark" detail and gives the member something to compare.

That contradicted Nevercott's 80 gp at the Yawning Portal in **The Wazoo Affair**. It also carried an invented "sixteen months" backstory that the Notable Figures page does not support. Both are gone. The event pays nothing, the pouches stay in **Coin Pouches**, and Krebbyg's history is limited to what his page says.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s04-contact-severed/design-notes.md:5: The restored draft restated an outcome the First Meeting already set ("Already set in **Trollskull Alley**") and spent its length listing what Jarlaxle would not do. The event now reads **BD Contact Severed** and never marks it. Two other events write the outcome. The **Bregan D'aerthe First Meeting** marks it for a character who gives Sergeant Dunmere their name or attacks Nevercott, and **Kreb Drops the Cover** marks it for a member who exposes the company's drow. This event is the one place that says what the outcome means, so a GM doesn't have to assemble the rule from three files.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s04-contact-severed/design-notes.md:9: The draft closed Bregan D'aerthe to the whole party after one report. Membership is individual in this campaign, so the event closes the door for the named character alone. A short block covers how a mixed party plays: members brief in private, a severed character can stand beside a member as a helping companion in public scenes, and **Kreb Drops the Cover** governs what a member may tell a severed friend. The block has been intentionally limited to the cases that come up at a table, which are the briefing, the booking office and the rank events. Each mission overview states its own companion rules.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s04-contact-severed/design-notes.md:13: The source says contact ends "for the time being" (`sources/adventure-wdh.json` near line 3600) and "for now" (`sources/Appendix_B_-_Player_Factions.md` near line 719). The remix guide and `player-factions-overview.md` say permanent. The event uses a rule a GM can apply without prep: the door is closed for the rest of Acts I through III. That keeps the source's softness, since nothing says the closure outlasts the Faire, and it matches the guide for every scene the Bregan D'aerthe events cover. **Vault of Dragons** is named as the first place anything could change, and the event doesn't write that scene.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s04-contact-severed/design-notes.md:17: The draft had the watchers vanish "two days" after the Watch visit. Each route gets its own opening beat and its own lines from Fel'rekt, and the first two learn nothing the company wouldn't have told them. The exposed member also gets the card back. The loss rule in the First Meeting says the mate refuses it, and a returned card is easier to run than a refusal that depends on the player visiting the ships.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s04-contact-severed/ev-01-contact-severed.md:235: > This Event runs for severed characters only. Bregan D'aerthe's missions, standalone events and rank events never fire for them. A severed character with no faction can still join another through its First Meeting: **Harpers First Meeting**, **Lords' Alliance First Meeting**, **Emerald Enclave First Meeting**, **Order of the Gauntlet: First Meeting**, **A Message from the Blackstaff** or **Doom Raiders First Meeting**. The rest of the party continues with whichever events its members and candidates have earned. This Event awards no Milestone Points.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s04-contact-severed/ev-01-contact-severed.md:239: A character who has been shut out of Bregan D'aerthe finds the watchers gone and the company's lieutenants unwilling to deal with them. Someone else in the city is willing to pay for what they know.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:5: **The Dockside Killer** leaves Soluun Xibrindas alive in two of its three outcomes, and Soluun's own page calls his disownment a cover story. Nothing settled what the company does about a drow agent seen killing in Waterdeep, and **The Compromised Eye** called the disownment real while its own background called it a cover. The user ruled that Jarlaxle expels him for real, because an agent seen killing openly in the city is a liability. This Event is where Bregan D'aerthe's members watch that ruling, so **The Compromised Eye**, **The Dive** and **Houseless Noble** can read an outcome and not an assumption.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:7: The Event is a standalone because no mission carries it. Its trigger is the calendar of **The Dockside Killer**, which a Bregan D'aerthe member may not have played. It awards no Renown because the ruling isn't a job, so every possible effect on a member is a loss or a choice.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:11: Soluun's disownment and his forged disc were built so the company could deny him, and the ruling makes the denial true. Soluun believes he is being tested, and Jarlaxle believes a zealot will stay a zealot. Jarlaxle is wrong, and that drives **The Dive**, where the expelled Soluun sells the *Scarlet Marpenoth*'s mooring. Planting the ruling here lets that mission read as the consequence of a scene the members attended.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:17: Nevercott is Jarlaxle in a *hat of disguise*, and nobody says "Jarlaxle" until **Jarlaxle Unmasked** is marked for a member. So the ruling has two versions: Nevercott's beside an empty chair, and Jarlaxle's own from the chair. The empty chair with a hat on its arm lets Nevercott speak for "the captain" without anyone explaining why the captain is absent.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:19: The Jarlaxle version runs only when every member present has the outcome, because a member who lacks it would see Nevercott vanish and Jarlaxle appear. At a mixed table the Nevercott version runs, and the member with the outcome sees through the hat in silence, as the organization page says the company treats the two identities.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:21: Soluun never calls Nevercott "captain" in the readaloud. He speaks of the captain in the third person and looks at the empty chair first. Members who don't know the identity learn nothing, and members who do notice that he knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:25: Krebbyg appears as himself only if **Kreb Unmasked** is marked for every member present, matching the rule that he drops the cover only in private. A member who has never seen him without the wig can't be shown a drow bookkeeper at midnight. Otherwise he is at the Seven Masks Theater running the evening house, and Fel'rekt does the work with two guards. The gate is table-wide for the same reason as the Jarlaxle gate: one scene can't show two faces to one audience.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:29: The *Scarlet Marpenoth* sits beneath the *Eyecatcher*, and a member who hasn't met Zardoz Zord would learn the ship's secret on stepping out of the cabin that holds the trapdoor. Blindfolds keep that secret from members who lack **Jarlaxle Unmasked**, and a DC 16 Perception check lets an attentive member infer the carnival without anyone confirming it. Members who have the outcome are rowed openly and enter through the captain's cabin, as on their earlier visit. Fel'rekt's reluctance with the blindfolds shows how little the company enjoys secrecy among its own.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:43: A Bregan D'aerthe member who stood in the alley of **The Dockside Killer** watched outsiders catch or kill the company's own agent. Decision 6 says the company knows within a tenday, Nevercott raises it once and coolly, and the penalty is 1 Renown. The beat is a short exchange in the corridor with a stated test, because a penalty set by a die roll alone would be a coin toss.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:51: If **Soluun Killed** was marked, the company has the Luskan sailors' description and Jarlaxle's interest turns personal. There is nothing to expel, so the scene shrinks to a conversation in which the speaker asks nothing that would force a member to betray a friend. Members come away knowing the company knows who the Doom Raiders' companions are, and **Sea Maidens Faire** picks that up when Jarlaxle meets the party. The witness beat runs here too, with the higher DC for the killing blow.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/design-notes.md:59: - **The Dockside Killer** describes the silver disc as a forged token whose knotwork a Bregan D'aerthe member can spot. This Event treats the forgery as the company's own work, struck at Jarlaxle's order so the company could deny Soluun, and doesn't name who made it.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/ev-01-the-killers-fate.md:18: > Soluun Xibrindas is the elder brother of Nar'l Xibrindas and a drow gunslinger who has served Jarlaxle with a zealot's devotion for years. He kills surface elves and half-elves because he was raised to. For some weeks he hunted them along the Dock Ward waterfront, choosing victims from the audiences of the Seven Masks Theater. Jarlaxle knew and hadn't decided what to do. The public story that Soluun was disowned for killing without orders was Jarlaxle's invention, put about so nobody could trace the killings to him. The silver disc at Soluun's neck is a forgery the company struck on purpose so it could deny him.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/ev-01-the-killers-fate.md:20: > Then Soluun was seen. The Doom Raiders stood in the alley, the Harbor Watch has his description, and the hired Luskan sailors who guarded the street end are talking in the quay taverns. An agent who kills openly in Waterdeep is a liability to every other agent, so Jarlaxle has decided to make the cover story true. Soluun is expelled tonight and leaves with nothing but his clothes, unless a member pleads for more.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s05-the-killers-fate/ev-01-the-killers-fate.md:22: > Soluun believes this is a test, or a mistake the captain will mend, and that belief is all that keeps him from spitting in Nevercott's face. It won't last. Jarlaxle thinks Soluun is too devoted to sell the company's secrets, and he is wrong. Soluun knows where the *Scarlet Marpenoth* lies and how it moves, and **The Dive** shows what he does with that after the captain refuses him. His brother Nar'l has spent two months burying Soluun's trail inside Xanathar's Guild, and **The Compromised Eye** shows what that cost.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m01-the-dockside-killer/design-notes.md:7: ***The playbill.*** The draft replaced the source's bloodstained Seven Masks playbill with a forged token. The playbill is back, with its DC 12 link to Bregan D'aerthe and its DC 14 note on "Rongquan Mystere" and Luskan money. It is the lead that **Faction Outposts** and **Sea Maidens Faire** read, and no Bregan D'aerthe event reads it. The token stays as a second item, because Soluun's Notable Figures page and **The Compromised Eye** both mention a forged Bregan D'aerthe token, which the company struck itself so it could deny him. Gorra's report of a Seven Masks ticket stub in a victim's boot and Heldar's account of *Blood Wedding* add two more routes to the theater, so the lead doesn't depend on one loot drop.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m01-the-dockside-killer/design-notes.md:13: ***The disownment.*** The restored draft treated Soluun's disownment as real. His Notable Figures page says it is a cover story and that Jarlaxle knows about the murders and hasn't decided what to do. This version keeps that in GM text only, and Soluun never mentions being disowned. He also never speaks of Lolth with devotion, because Bregan D'aerthe rejects her: when asked about his goddess he answers with contempt.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m01-the-dockside-killer/design-notes.md:15: ***Hired hands.*** A lone Scout is no threat to a party of three, four or five, and the mechanics reference recommends dock hirelings for the flanks. Two Luskan sailors, a **Bandit** and a **Tough** depending on party size, hold the Ship Street corner and close the alley mouth. They never learn Soluun's name, so Bregan D'aerthe muscle stays out of a mission where Soluun acts on his own.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m01-the-dockside-killer/design-notes.md:25: ***Nar'l Xibrindas.*** Nar'l is Soluun's brother and Jarlaxle's asset inside Xanathar's Guild. The party doesn't learn this here. **The Compromised Eye** pays it off, and Krebbyg refers to this encounter for parties that ran both chains.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m01-the-dockside-killer/design-notes.md:27: ***Davil's silence.*** Davil says "That's interesting" and nothing more. His profile says he never explains why he wants to know something, and the players' curiosity becomes a seed for later Bregan D'aerthe encounters.

campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:83: > - **Who knows (secrecy rule):** Nobody, before the party finds out. Every faction is suspicious of the Cassalanters because they are too nice to be this rich and this purebred, and none knows about the pact, the soul contract or the infernalism. The one exception is Jarlaxle, who knows through Vessa, his doppelganger spy in the Cassalanter social circle. He tells Bregan D'aerthe members nothing unless they work it out. The Emerald Enclave senses infernal influence in the Sea Ward through Jeryth and does not know whose it is. Savra Belabranta of the Order of the Gauntlet hunts infernal cults in Waterdeep and wants to learn what the Cassalanters conceal, which is suspicion only.

No member worships her, keeps a shrine to her or invokes the Spider Queen with devotion, and members speak of her with contempt, as the goddess they walked away from. Do not give any company member a shrine, a prayer or a kind word for her.

campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:23: If the party refused or turned Hostile, the campaign takes a harder shape. This may push Jarlaxle to propose an alliance against the Cassalanters.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:59: **Escalation:** Cassalanters move to Alert (Tier 3). Imp survivors report to the family within hours; Seffia's team deploys within 2 days. If Bregan D'aerthe outposts have also been hit, the Cassalanters flag the party as a threat to their Faire surveillance arrangement. **Founders' Day Clock (Flamerule 1): 1–2 days.** The clock is the number of in-game days the operation takes out of the time left before Founders' Day; the Windmill's 3–5 days is counted the same way.

campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:10: > - **Quests and events:** **Finding Floon** opens in the Yawning Portal, where a Bregan D'aerthe observer watches the well (**The Yawning Portal**). **Trollskull Alley** plants three Undermountain seeds in **The Grand Opening**. **Xanathar's Lair** runs a tunnel to Skullport, and **Vault of Dragons** ends in the Undermountain handoff. The faction events that reach into the dungeon are **The Grells in the Dock Ward**, **Autumnreaver** and **The Dreamer's Reach** (Emerald Enclave), **The Stone's Other Master** (Harpers), **Lioncrown** (Lords' Alliance), **Houseless Noble** (Bregan D'aerthe) and **Force Grey Commander** (Force Grey).
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:111: > - **Bregan D'aerthe's eyes (campaign only):** A drow woman at the Yawning Portal's balcony table "has been at that table every evening for two weeks, cataloguing who descends into Undermountain and who comes back" (`campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:29`). She is **Ryvarra**, and the Finding Floon design notes say that Jarlaxle has had eyes on the party "since their first night in the city" (`design-notes.md:11`). The company's Skullport drop in **Houseless Noble** is a ropewalk on the lower quay: "Xanathar's Guild holds the whole place, so say nothing of us in the street" (`campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/ev-01-houseless-noble.md:284`).
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:143: - **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim**, **Bregan D'aerthe**, **Emerald Enclave**, **Lords' Alliance**, **Harpers** and **Force Grey**: the factions with a foothold or a quest.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:86: > - **Faction benefits that name the Watch:** A Gray Hand is Friendly to any Watch officer who sees the letter of authorization, and an officer who has watched the member commit a crime is no longer Friendly. A Stingblade can read the Watch's files, which takes one hour for standard files and four hours and a DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion) check for classified ones. A High Harper can ask Mirt for Watch cooperation, which delivers a sealed instruction to the responsible station three days after Mirt accepts, gives access to the relevant records and assigns a liaison, and raises no new squad. A Lords' Alliance member earns 1 renown, once per incident, for helping the Watch in a crisis beyond its capacity. A Bregan D'aerthe Commander can arrange passage in or out of Waterdeep without the Watch's knowledge once per quest.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/guilds.md:70: > - **The Dungsweepers' Guild:** In the published adventure, members of the guild or of Xanathar's Guild erase the chalk symbols in the sewers, and four Dungsweepers carrying intellect devourers visit the tavern on the second night of a Bregan D'aerthe renown mission. The campaign builds on both. In **The Long Watch**, Delvon Harsk sells the crew's timings to Pirn, a street agent of Xanathar's Guild, to pay a 300 gp debt, and Berna's crew is clean. In **Three Nights**, four Dungsweepers' workers sit down at the back table in the evening and say nothing. In **The Exile's Return**, a Dungsweepers' night man is one of three sources who name the rope warehouse in Mudwagon Court (campaign-only).
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/guilds.md:71: > - **Bregan D'aerthe in the guilds:** Fenerus Stormcastle is a lamplighter by day and a Bregan D'aerthe cut-out at night. His Notable Figures page says he believes his contacts are Luskan agents, and the Bregan D'aerthe page says he once followed Krebbyg and knows the messages go to the **Sea Maidens Faire**. Among the messages that Krebbyg collects from his house is a note about the Council of Musicians' objection to the Faire, resolved through the Jesters' Guild, and another that ends "We have eyes up in the Watchful Order." (campaign-only).
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/guilds.md:105: - **The Lords' Alliance**, **The Harpers**, **The Emerald Enclave**, **The Order of the Gauntlet**, **The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim**, **Bregan D'aerthe**, **Xanathar's Guild** and **The Cassalanters** (Organizations): the factions tied to individual guilds.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/guilds.md:120: > - **Conflicts, Watchful Order:** the adventure lists it among the guilds (line 1157) and treats it as a body that casters must register with (lines 1101 and 30578). The wiki says it formed in 1318 DR on the Guilds page (`wiki.txt` line 183) and 1317 DR on the Watchful Order page (`city-watch/wiki.txt` line 313), and gives dated leaders including Bowgentra Summertaen (c. 1491 DR). `fireball/ev-03-the-nimblewright-hunt.md` line 28 puts a Bowgentra Summertaen in the Castle Ward as a household servant, and the Bregan D'aerthe message (`08-bregan-daerthe.md` line 93) delivers a nimblewright to her and says "We have eyes up in the Watchful Order". Nothing says the same person is meant. Vajra's page does not mention the Order. The page follows the adventure and the campaign (the Blackstaff heads it) and does not name Bowgentra. `[research]`
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:69: > - **What a party can ask of a Lord:** At **Warduke** a member sends one written request per quest to a Masked Lord whom Jalester Silvermane will not name. The Lord is never named, and the Lord is neither Mirt nor Jelenn. At **Commander** a member of Bregan D'aerthe can ask for a private audience with a Masked Lord. The four Lords the company can reach are Mirt, Durnan, Ulkoria and Jelenn, and Zardoz never confirms any of them aloud.

campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:1: # Bregan D'aerthe's Long Road
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:8: > - **What this is:** The Era page for how Jarlaxle Baenre and Bregan D'aerthe came from Menzoberranzan to Luskan and then to Waterdeep. It is the third of eight eras, after **The Long Shadow** and before **The Founding of Chains**.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** The GM reads it before voicing Jarlaxle, Soluun, Krebbyg or Nar'l, before the Bregan D'aerthe recruitment visit in **Trollskull Alley**, and before any player asks where the company comes from or why a carnival captain is interested in Waterdeep.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:11: > - **Spoiler level:** Everything above the Overview is GM-only. The Overview is the public face and links neither Captain Zardoz Zord nor Luskan's rulers to the company's leader.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:13: **Purpose:** Lets the GM answer any question about who founded Bregan D'aerthe, why its members reject Lolth, how it came to dominate Luskan, and what Jarlaxle had in place before the campaign opens.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:15: **Keywords:** Bregan D'aerthe, Jarlaxle Baenre, Zardoz Zord, Yvonnel Baenre, Menzoberranzan, Lolth, Luskan, High Captains, Lords' Alliance, Sea Maidens Faire, Lantan, Scarlet Marpenoth
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:17: **Span:** The 10th century DR to 1492 DR, about five centuries. The Forgotten Realms Wiki has Jarlaxle starting the company slowly in the 10th century DR, and a line about fifteen decades of the company by 1118 DR implies about 968 DR. The second figure is derived. `[research]` The old era block said "decades", which is wrong. The surface chapter runs from 1366 DR (a brief base in Calimport) through 1377 DR (a major presence in Luskan) to after 1485 DR (the High Captains under Jarlaxle's sway). The campaign opens on Ches 1, 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:19: **Other Names:** Jarlaxle's other names are GM names: Jarlaxle Baenre, Jarlaxle D'aerthe (a wiki alias), Captain Zardoz Zord (the carnival master), J.B. Nevercott (the haberdasher who visits Trollskull Manor, the party's headquarters; `adventure-wdh.json` line 3601) and Rongquan Mystere (a disguise at the Shipwright's Ball; `adventure-wdh.json` lines 281 and 9066-9075). The old block translates the company's name as "those of the broken house". No source gives a translation. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:24: > - Jarlaxle was born to Matron Mother Yvonnel Baenre of House Baenre, the first house of Menzoberranzan. He survived his sacrifice to Lolth and later rejected her. Bregan D'aerthe rejects her too, and its members speak of her with contempt.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:27: > - Luskan is ruled by five High Captains, and after 1485 DR they are under the sway of Jarlaxle and the company. The published adventure has him declaring himself its secret lord.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:33: - Jarlaxle was born to Yvonnel Baenre, the Matron Mother of House Baenre. The wiki cites Road of the Patriarch, Menzoberranzan: City of Intrigue and the Forgotten Realms Campaign Setting for his parentage, and the published adventure gives his surname as Baenre.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:36: - This is where the campaign rule starts. Jarlaxle rejected Lolth, Bregan D'aerthe rejects her, and its members speak of her as the goddess they walked away from. Soluun gives Jarlaxle the devotion that a drow of the houses would give the Spider Queen.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:38: - A campaign docx calls him the third son, and the page keeps that. The wiki gives no number, but it calls Doquaio Yvonnel's second-oldest son and says Jarlaxle was slated for sacrifice because of his birth order, which implies at least a third son (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Jarlaxle Baenre; Road of the Patriarch).
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:42: - Jarlaxle left his house and its name, though not his connections, and founded Bregan D'aerthe.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:44: - A female drow can earn a place by decrying the matriarchy and convincing Jarlaxle that she would be an asset. He also employs non-drow operatives, none of whom know they work for him and none of whom count as members.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:47: - The old block says Jarlaxle turned disposability into leverage. The wiki gives the sense without saying it was a founding act: a male drow survives best by standing outside the system and being too valuable to eliminate. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:53: - The move to the surface was an expansion for influence and for trade with Menzoberranzan. The old block calls it strategic and not desperate. The wiki ties the first base, in Calimport in 1366 DR, in part to the influence of the artifact Crenshinibon on Jarlaxle. Draft Notes logs both versions. `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:62: - After 1485 DR the High Captains trade legally across the Sword Coast and are secretly under the sway of Jarlaxle and the company. The published adventure says he infiltrated Luskan, brought a kind of order to its lawlessness and declared himself its secret lord.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:64: - Lords' Alliance membership is the latest of Jarlaxle's schemes in the published adventure, and Luskan's unsavory reputation has thwarted every earlier attempt. The Alliance's current leaders have voiced their opposition, and a few have declared that Luskan will never be welcome. He aims to persuade Laeral to champion the city, even if it costs the Alliance other members. The campaign makes this his main aim in Waterdeep (see **Jarlaxle Baenre** in Villains).
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:72: - The Sea Maidens Faire already exists as one of Jarlaxle's legitimate business enterprises. It is a traveling carnival based in Luskan that tours the Sword Coast in three ships, the *Eyecatcher*, the *Heartbreaker* and the *Hellraiser*, under Captain Zardoz Zord. Zord is Jarlaxle in the magical guise of an Illuskan human.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:73: - Each ship has its own captain, a drow mage in the guise of a human, and all of them know Jarlaxle's plans. They keep in touch with him by *Sending*.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:74: - Zord visits Lantan about once a year. On his last visit he bought nimblewrights from a Lantanese wizard. Jarlaxle has an alliance with Lantan, arms his lieutenants with Lantanese firearms that run on smokepowder, and keeps a Lantanese submarine, the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. The published adventure mounts it below the *Eyecatcher*. In the campaign it has been moored in Deepwater Harbor since he reached the city, and the carnival fleet joins it for Fleetswake. He plans to flee in it if his scheme unravels.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:75: - Bregan D'aerthe is skilled at infiltrating criminal organizations. Nar'l Xibrindas became Xanathar's only advisor and persuaded it to eliminate its other advisors. He has been in place one year, since 1491 DR.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:76: - Before the campaign opens, Jarlaxle has an agent inside Xanathar's Guild and the *Scarlet Marpenoth* moored in Deepwater Harbor. The Faire's carnival fleet and its parades arrive in the harbor on Ches 21 for Fleetswake, Ches 21 to 30. He arrives in Waterdeep already knowing of the Stone, the three Eyes and the vault, and Nar'l's reports keep him current. The nimblewright ledger puts him in the city no later than Marpenoth 3, 1491 DR. Eye #3 reaches him aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* on the night before Ches 1, lifted from Xanathar's hideout by agents already inside it (see **The Open Lord's Fall**).
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:81: Bregan D'aerthe is a drow mercenary company that began in the Underdark city of Menzoberranzan, made up mostly of men from houses that were destroyed or disgraced. It hires out soldiers, scouts and go-betweens, and its agents are said to deal in information with anyone who can pay. On the surface it is said to have a foothold in Luskan, a free port on the northern Sword Coast that five High Captains rule and that Waterdeep regards as a pirate state. This spring the Sea Maidens Faire, a traveling carnival out of Luskan, brings three ships and Captain Zardoz Zord to Deepwater Harbor for Fleetswake.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:86: > - Captain Zardoz Zord is Jarlaxle Baenre, and he is the secret lord of Luskan. Nothing in the Overview links him to the company, and the party learns the link in **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:87: > - Jarlaxle holds Eye #3 of the Stone of Golorr aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, and he is the only outsider who knows what the Cassalanters have done (see **A Debt in Blood**). He does not tell Bregan D'aerthe members unless they work it out themselves.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:88: > - Nar'l Xibrindas reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending*, and Xanathar does not know it.

campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:141: > - **The nimblewrights.** The campaign has Jarlaxle selling nimblewrights to wealthy households as an intelligence network (`arc-h` line 13), and the published adventure has him buying them from a Lantanese wizard (`adventure-wdh.json` line 4746). campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:151: > - **Research:** Yvonnel, Doquaio and Gromph Baenre, Arathis Hune, Beniago and Kimmuriel Oblodra are wiki names with no campaign page. campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:154: > - **Conflicts:** Jarlaxle "born illegitimate" (`history.md` line 30). Every source names Yvonnel Baenre as his mother (`Jarlaxle_Baenre.txt` lines 37 and 96), and the published adventure names him Baenre. The page drops "illegitimate".
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:160: > - **Settled by the user (Session 47):** When Jarlaxle learned of the vault. "He's known about it since getting to Waterdeep, whenever that was." The body has him arrive already knowing, and the nimblewright ledger (first sale Marpenoth 3, 1491 DR) puts his arrival no later than that. The **Jarlaxle Informed** outcome set in **Fireball!** (`villains/jarlaxle.md` lines 3 and 13) records only that the party spoke with him. campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:16: The Stone of Golorr is an aboleth that magic has turned into an artifact, and it holds the location of the Vault of Dragons. Dagult Neverember blinded it by removing three gemstone Eyes and putting them in three separate hands, so that a thief who stole the Stone alone would learn nothing. When the campaign opens, Xanathar holds one Eye, the Cassalanters hold one and Jarlaxle holds one.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:43: - **Eye #3, Jarlaxle:** It began in Renaer's locket. Manshoon's cell seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse. Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried the Eye off with the rest of the back office, and nobody in the Guild recognized it. Bregan D'aerthe agents posing as guards lifted it from a strongbox in the hideout before dawn, and it reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:44: - **Where Jarlaxle keeps it:** In U4 aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, in a velvet-lined compartment at the base of the crystal ball pedestal that runs his nimblewright network. Jarlaxle knows exactly what it is.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:46: - **Who needs what:** Xanathar's Guild wants the Stone and does not know what the Eyes are for. The Cassalanters need the Stone and Eyes #1 and #3. Bregan D'aerthe needs the Stone and Eyes #1 and #2.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:104: - **Bregan D'aerthe:** Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he reached Waterdeep, and he holds Eye #3.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:1: # Jarlaxle Baenre / "Zardoz Zord"
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:12: **Resonance.** Jarlaxle should inspire delight with his theatrical flair and razor wit, unease with the realization that every conversation is a negotiation, and reluctant admiration for an opponent who prefers confused enemies to dead ones.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:14: **Emotions.** Jarlaxle most often feels theatrical amusement, predatory curiosity, strategic calculation behind every smile, honest delight when a scheme comes together, contempt for fools who threaten him (swiftly expressed), and possessive loyalty to Bregan D'Aerthe that he frames as pragmatism.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:16: **Motivations.** Jarlaxle wants to buy Luskan's way into the Lords' Alliance using the dragon hoard, acquire the dragonstaff of Ahghairon for leverage over Waterdeep, and ensure that if everything falls apart, the submarine is fueled and ready.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:18: **Inspirations.** When playing Jarlaxle, channel Jack Sparrow (*Pirates of the Caribbean*), Loki (*Marvel*), and Hector (*Westworld*).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Jarlaxle is Captain Zardoz Zord, a flamboyant human sea captain running the Sea Maidens Faire carnival. To those he trusts, he is the secret lord of Luskan, the commander of Bregan D'Aerthe, and a drow iconoclast whose loyalties run to himself first and his operatives second. Deep down, he likes to weave schemes that leave his enemies baffled, and the look on their faces when he gets the better of them is its own reward. The first thing he does with new contacts is determine whether they are useful or dangerous. Ideally, he discovers they are both.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:24: **Morale.** In combat, Jarlaxle would swiftly and brutally kill someone to make an example, then propose terms. He reaches for leverage before force. If reduced to danger, he uses his wealth of magic items to escape. Direct confrontation only comes when the PCs kill his lieutenants without first offering terms.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:26: **Relationships.** Jarlaxle is the leader of Bregan D'Aerthe, the secret lord of Luskan, the employer of Soluun, Fel'rekt, and Krebbyg, and a rival who may become an ally if the party offers him something worth more than the gold. He has known of the Stone, the Eyes, and the vault since reaching Waterdeep, no later than Marpenoth 3, 1491 DR. He alone outside the household and the cult knows of the Cassalanter pact, through his doppelganger spy Vessa. Eye #3 is aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/02-soluun-xibrindas.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/02-soluun-xibrindas.md:12: **Resonance.** Soluun should inspire revulsion with his methodical murder of surface elves, discomfort with his fanatical loyalty to Jarlaxle, and pity for the hatred that defines his entire existence.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/02-soluun-xibrindas.md:16: **Motivations.** Soluun wants to kill every surface elf and half-elf he can find without consequences, serve Jarlaxle with absolute loyalty, and prove that his brother Nar'l is the weak one.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/02-soluun-xibrindas.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Soluun is a drow gunslinger of Bregan D'Aerthe, disowned for unsanctioned Dock Ward murders. To those he trusts, he is fiercely loyal to Jarlaxle and Bregan D'Aerthe, and he considers his brother Nar'l a weakling who turned to arcane magic by way of compensation. Deep down, he was taught from a young age to kill surface elves as opportunity permits, and the habit has become his identity. He despises Lolth and the matron houses that serve her, as the whole band does, and the devotion a drow of the houses would give the Spider Queen he gives to Jarlaxle instead.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/02-soluun-xibrindas.md:24: **Morale.** In a fight, Soluun attacks without hesitation or mercy. He carries a bloodstained Seven Masks Theater playbill for Blood Wedding; he stalks and murders elven and half-elven audience members after shows. Jarlaxle is aware and hasn't yet decided what to do about it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/02-soluun-xibrindas.md:26: **Relationships.** Soluun is the elder brother of Nar'l Xibrindas (whom he despises), a lieutenant of Jarlaxle (whom he worships), and a serial killer whose disownment by Bregan D'Aerthe is a cover story: he carries forged BD identification.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/03-felrekt-lafeen.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/03-felrekt-lafeen.md:14: **Emotions.** Fel'rekt most often feels determined optimism, competitive enthusiasm, easy camaraderie with Krebbyg, genuine gratitude toward Jarlaxle, restless eagerness, and a stubborn refusal to let cruelty define him.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/03-felrekt-lafeen.md:16: **Motivations.** Fel'rekt wants to prove himself worthy of Jarlaxle's confidence, protect Krebbyg (his closest friend), and demonstrate that leaving drow society made him stronger rather than weaker.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/03-felrekt-lafeen.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Fel'rekt is a drow gunslinger, one of Jarlaxle's most trusted lieutenants. To those he trusts, he was born female in a society that punishes male drow; he petitioned to join Bregan D'Aerthe and Jarlaxle took an immediate shine to him. Deep down, he lacks the cruelty common to most drow and will not kill an adversary unless left with no other choice.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/03-felrekt-lafeen.md:26: **Relationships.** Fel'rekt is one of Jarlaxle's most loyal lieutenants, the close friend and operational partner of Krebbyg Masq'il'yr, and the Bregan D'Aerthe operative who led the team at Gralhund Villa.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/03-felrekt-lafeen.md:30: A drow gunslinger and Jarlaxle's trusted lieutenant — eager to prove himself, reluctant to kill when any other option remains, and almost always accompanied by Krebbyg.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/04-krebbyg-masqilyr.md:26: **Relationships.** Krebbyg is the close friend and operational partner of Fel'rekt Lafeen, Jarlaxle's logistics lieutenant (collecting messages from Fenerus Stormcastle for the Scarlet Marpenoth), and the operating cover manager of the Seven Masks Theater.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/05-zelifarn.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** None (friendly to Bregan D'aerthe; not an operative)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/05-zelifarn.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Zelifarn is a young bronze dragon who surfaces near ships in Deepwater Harbor with bronze-scaled curiosity and a cheerful wave. To those who engage with him honestly, he will trade specific information about the Eyecatcher for specific information about the surface world, and the reward he offers is genuine and already prepared. Deep down, he does not report to Jarlaxle or anyone else. He knows the *Eyecatcher* has something under her hull and that her crew discouraged him from looking, but he does not know whose the vessel is. He never lies, and he trades facts for facts.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/07-malcolm-brizzenbright.md:12: **Resonance.** Malcolm should inspire pity for a man who loved his theater enough to haunt it; endearment with his fussy pride in the building's artistic integrity; and useful surprise when his grievance against Jarlaxle turns out to be the intelligence the party needed.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/07-malcolm-brizzenbright.md:26: **Relationships.** Malcolm Brizzenbright is the founding ghost of the Seven Masks Theater, the only witness to the secret door between the theater and the Vault of Dragons, and a man whose handwriting analysis of Jarlaxle's correspondence is reliable to anyone with a Harper contact who has seen the original letters.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/08-quilm.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Quilm is a drow who applied for the bouncer position with a handwritten recommendation and does the job without comment or complaint. To those who read the recommendation letter carefully, the handwriting is recognizably Jarlaxle's — the flourishes are distinctive, visible to any drow character or anyone who has previously seen his correspondence (DC 12 Investigation). Deep down, he filed his first intelligence report the evening he was hired and has not missed a week.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/08-quilm.md:26: **Relationships.** Quilm is a Bregan D'Aerthe scout placed as a Trollskull Manor bouncer candidate, the reason no Bregan D'Aerthe response team visit materializes while he works there, a man whose recommendation from "J" contains Jarlaxle's handwriting, and a weekly intelligence source for Jarlaxle's Waterdeep network.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/09-ryvarra.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/09-ryvarra.md:12: **Resonance.** Ryvarra should inspire the particular unease of someone who has been cataloguing the party since before they knew they were interesting; quiet admiration for operational patience executed over months without slipping; and retroactive dread once the party understands that every move they made in the Yawning Portal was already filed and addressed to Jarlaxle.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/09-ryvarra.md:14: **Emotions.** Ryvarra most often feels analytical detachment, the practiced stillness of someone trained to read a room without being read, professional loyalty to Jarlaxle, private curiosity about the party (they are more interesting than most of what she observes), and a controlled impatience she has not let surface in two weeks of watching the well.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/09-ryvarra.md:16: **Motivations.** Ryvarra wants to file accurate weekly reports to Jarlaxle on Undermountain traffic, maintain her cover as a Calimshan cloth merchant for as long as operationally viable, and avoid any action that would compromise Bregan D'Aerthe's Waterdeep network.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/09-ryvarra.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Ryvarra is a Calimshan cloth merchant taking her evening wine at a reserved table while trade negotiations drag on. To those who observe carefully, she never drinks from the glass, never takes notes, and her Calimshan accent slips in ways a trained ear notices (DC 14 Insight). Deep down, she has been filing weekly intelligence reports to Jarlaxle since her first night in the city, and her current dispatch names the party by description, details the brawl and the troll fight, and flags them as potential long-term variables.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/09-ryvarra.md:26: **Relationships.** Ryvarra is a Bregan D'Aerthe field observer stationed at the Yawning Portal to catalog Undermountain traffic, a weekly intelligence source for Jarlaxle's Waterdeep network, and the first evidence (retroactively understood) that Jarlaxle had eyes on the party from their first night in the city.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/10-margo-verida.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Bregan D'aerthe (Jarlaxle's companion, not his employee)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/10-margo-verida.md:14: **Emotions.** Margo most often feels creative absorption during a performance, relaxed warmth in Jarlaxle's company, quick professional assessment of new people's motives, genuine pleasure in a room that does not know what it is looking at, and a clean loyalty to Jarlaxle that has nothing servile about it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/10-margo-verida.md:16: **Motivations.** Margo wants to keep making music worth making, maintain her friendship with Jarlaxle on the terms they have established, and be honest about what she is — a bard and a skilled combatant who chooses her associates freely and has chosen well.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/10-margo-verida.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Margo Verida is a performer traveling with Zardoz Zord's carnival — excellent, clearly at home on a ship, someone you assume is in Jarlaxle's company for professional reasons. To those who deal with her directly, she is one of the few people Jarlaxle treats as a peer rather than an asset, and she notices when the treatment shifts. Deep down, she flattery-tested Jarlaxle once, early in the acquaintance, to confirm his compliments were genuine rather than operational; they were, which is why she is still here.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/10-margo-verida.md:24: **Morale.** In a fight, Margo uses bardic magic for control and positioning before engaging directly. She is not suicidal and is not Jarlaxle's shield; she fights for reasons she has chosen, not on assignment. A DC 13 Persuasion check built on specific flattery about her music opens a social window before any fight becomes unavoidable. She will not betray Jarlaxle under any circumstances.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/10-margo-verida.md:26: **Relationships.** Margo is Jarlaxle's companion aboard the Eyecatcher — not his employee, his friend — paired with Khafeyta Murzan in J29, a BD-associated operative whose affiliation derives from freely chosen loyalty rather than organizational membership, and a combatant significantly more capable than a performer's cabin assignment implies.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/10-margo-verida.md:30: A bard performing aboard Zardoz Zord's carnival ships — the music is genuinely excellent, and the woman playing it is a good deal more capable in a fight than her cabin assignment implies.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/11-khafeyta-murzan.md:16: **Motivations.** Khafeyta wants to be excellent at her craft, spend time with people worth spending it with, and find situations complicated enough to be interesting. She is the more aggressive of the two companions, the one who steps forward while Margo controls the room behind her. A DC 14 Persuasion check grounded in a credible account of a real fight creates a social opening before violence becomes the only option. She will not betray Jarlaxle.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/11-khafeyta-murzan.md:26: **Relationships.** Khafeyta is Jarlaxle's companion aboard the Eyecatcher, paired with Margo Verida in J29, a swashbuckler whose presence alongside Margo means J29 is significantly harder to pass through uncontested than its designation implies.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/11-khafeyta-murzan.md:30: A fighter traveling with Zardoz Zord's carnival who carries herself like someone who knows exactly how good she is — her assessment of the current room's exits and weapons is finished before the pleasantries are.

His hostile-mode escalation: he attempts to assassinate Jarlaxle, fails, then targets the PCs in a follow-up.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/04-filthy-meg.md:26: **Relationships.** Filthy Meg is an independent merfolk information broker operating out of Mistshore — she is not on Xanathar's payroll and not a BD asset, which is precisely what makes her valuable. She has existing relationships with both Jarlaxle (who gave her a ring of true sight as a retainer payment and gets harbor intelligence in return) and the Cassalanters (who have paid for Field Ward surveillance twice). Her network of homeless residents in the Field Ward is her primary collection mechanism: they notice who passes through, where people sleep, and what disappears. She resurfaces in **Sea Maidens Faire** as the best independent source on the Sea Maidens Faire's crew movements and in **Vault of Dragons** on Brandath Crypt foot traffic.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/01-xanathar.md:27: **Relationships.** Xanathar is the beholder crime lord of Xanathar's Guild, the employer of Ahmaergo (its majordomo), Nihiloor (its mind flayer, which has psionically dominated Xanathar for the last four months without Xanathar knowing), and Nar'l Xibrindas (its drow advisor, whom it does not yet know is Jarlaxle's spy). It has a healthy fear of Laeral Silverhand and spares those in her employ.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:14: **Emotions.** Nar'l most often feels nervous calculation, growing paranoia, practiced competence masking terror, contempt for Xanathar's delusions, desperate self-preservation, and bitter awareness that Jarlaxle would sacrifice him without hesitation if the cost were right.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:16: **Motivations.** Nar'l wants to survive his assignment as Jarlaxle's intelligence asset inside Xanathar's Guild, avoid Xanathar's suspicion (which is growing), and keep his options open in case he needs to betray Bregan D'Aerthe to save himself.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Nar'l is Xanathar's trusted drow advisor who convinced the beholder to eliminate its other counselors. To Jarlaxle, he is Bregan D'Aerthe's best intelligence source on Guild movements, reporting via Sending spell after one year in place, placed in 1491 DR. Deep down, Xanathar suspects a mole and has assigned Nar'l a grell bodyguard with orders to kill him at the first sign of disloyalty. If forced to choose between himself and Bregan D'Aerthe, he will choose himself.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:26: **Relationships.** Nar'l is the younger brother of Soluun Xibrindas (who considers him a weakling), Jarlaxle's intelligence asset inside Xanathar's Guild, and Xanathar's only advisor, who has a grell bodyguard assigned to execute him at the first sign of treachery.

campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:1: # Bregan D'aerthe
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:7: > - **Contacts:** **Jarlaxle Baenre** / "Zardoz Zord" (primary, Sea Maidens Faire); **Krebbyg Masq'il'yr** / "Kreb Sorrush" (street contact, Seven Masks Theater)
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:8: > - **Mission delivery:** Mission 1 by unsigned theater tickets that lead to Kreb Sorrush at the Seven Masks; Mission 2 by J.B. Nevercott in person at the Yawning Portal; Missions 3–6 in person through Krebbyg.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:11: > **Primary Contact:** Jarlaxle Baenre — operating as "Zardoz Zord," a flamboyant Illuskan sea captain running the Sea Maidens Faire out of three carnival ships in Deepwater Harbor. His first in-person meeting with the party is the Trollskull Alley recruitment visit, arriving as "J.B. Nevercott," a haberdasher who happens to know a great deal about editorial publishing schedules. Mission 2 (The Wazoo Affair) is the first job he brings after that meeting. He does not acknowledge that J.B. Nevercott and Zardoz Zord are the same person, even when both have been seen by the same party member — this is the kind of thing he finds amusing. After Mission 4, he dispenses with J.B. Nevercott but remains Zardoz Zord until **Sea Maidens Faire**, where the party meets the real Jarlaxle Baenre.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:19: Like the city's other factions, Bregan D'aerthe recruits quietly, watching candidates before approaching them.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:21: Bregan D'aerthe is a mercenary company of drow exiles and surface operatives, led by Jarlaxle Baenre, who has been outmaneuvering Underdark politics for generations. In Waterdeep, the operation runs out of the Sea Maidens Faire — a traveling carnival that is exactly what it appears to be, and also something else entirely. Jarlaxle does not brawl in the streets. He acquires information, sells it selectively, positions operatives in every major faction, and acts decisively only when the odds are already arranged in his favor.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:23: Joining Bregan D'aerthe means working for Jarlaxle, who genuinely values capable people and will treat his agents well — until their interests diverge from his, at which point he will maneuver them into serving his ends anyway, with a smile. Characters who join this faction are playing a game where the other player is better at it than they are. Pay attention.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:25: **Primary Contact:** Jarlaxle Baenre — operating in Waterdeep as the flamboyant Illuskan carnival master Zardoz Zord. He is always performing, but the performance is genuine: a man who chose delight as a survival strategy and discovered it works. He gives compliments that are precise and earned. He remembers everything you said three conversations ago. He is never threatened by capable people — he collects them. The tell players should learn to watch for: when Jarlaxle stops smiling, the situation has become genuinely serious.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:29: Jarlaxle Baenre is many things: the secret lord of Luskan, the commander of Bregan D'Aerthe, and in Waterdeep, Captain Zardoz Zord of the Sea Maidens Faire, a traveling carnival that covers his intelligence operations. He arrived in the city to advance Luskan's bid to join the Lords' Alliance, and came prepared: nimblewrights sold to wealthy families across every ward, each one harvesting intelligence through a crystal ball aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:31: He is present from **Trollskull Alley** onward, operating behind J.B. Nevercott's haberdasher fiction while Zardoz Zord runs the carnival. The nimblewright investigation in **Fireball!** is what leads the party to the man behind both names — from that point he stops pretending the party is just clientele. A conversation with Jarlaxle is rarely what it appears to be. The first thing he'll do is determine whether the PCs are useful or dangerous. Ideally, he'll discover they're both.

campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:46: - **Vessa** — Doppelganger operative embedded in the Cassalanter social scene under a permanent human cover; Jarlaxle's primary intelligence source on the Cassalanter operation throughout **Cassalanter Villa**. See the **Vessa** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:50: Jarlaxle wants to return the gold to Laeral Silverhand — making the Open Lord personally indebted to him, which is worth more than the gold itself in his long-running project to secure Luskan's admission to the Lords' Alliance. He also wants the Stone of Golorr for Underdark political reasons that extend well beyond the surface campaign. PCs who understand his actual goals can deal with him openly and find him a reliable, if unsettling, partner. PCs who mistake him for a simple villain will be outmaneuvered at every turn — and will enjoy it considerably less than if they had paid attention.

campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:58: Bregan D'aerthe is a mercenary company of drow exiles and surface operatives, led by Jarlaxle Baenre, running the Sea Maidens Faire carnival as cover for a city-wide intelligence network. He has positioned his people inside every major faction in Waterdeep. Joining him means working for the most capable operator in the city — one who will treat his agents well until their interests diverge from his.

campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:1: # Jarlaxle Baenre
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:3: > **Entry and Activation:** Two separate mechanics govern Bregan D'aerthe in this campaign. **Membership** runs during **Trollskull Alley**, like any other faction: drow lieutenants shadow the party for several days, then J.B. Nevercott (Jarlaxle in disguise, posing as a haberdasher) visits Trollskull Manor and offers enrollment to any interested PC. The only gate is party action — if the party reported the watchers to the Watch, the **BD Contact Severed** flag closes the faction for the campaign. The **Ryvarra Identified** flag (from **The Neighbors** event in **Trollskull Alley**) changes how the visit opens, not whether it happens. **Villain activation** is a separate track: Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep, and he enters the Grand Game as a named player at the table when the **Jarlaxle Informed** flag is set during **Fireball!** and read in **Gralhund Villa**. The flag records only that the party spoke directly with him. It does not mean he learned anything, and it gates his Bregan D'aerthe team at **Gralhund Villa** and his Phase 1 Zardoz Zord activity. His identity stays hidden behind J.B. Nevercott and Zardoz Zord until **Sea Maidens Faire**. The personality phases below key off the Jarlaxle Informed flag, not BD membership. See: **GM Guide: Session Zero**; **GM Guide: Player Factions Overview**; **Bregan D'aerthe** organization page.
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:9: *Phase 1 (universal) — Zardoz Zord*

campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:57: 2. **One Eye of the Stone** — as a bargaining chip, not for personal use; method: he already holds Eye #3 aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* (Bregan D'aerthe agents inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the night Xanathar's Guild raided the Zhentarim warehouse), and Nar'l's intelligence tells him where the other Eyes are; keep it as the bargaining chip and move on the others before the other factions consolidate; contingency: negotiating it from the PCs is also acceptable and preferable for the relationship; connects to **Sea Maidens Faire**
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:58: 3. **Waterdeep as Permanent Base** — the Sea Maidens Faire is seasonal; Jarlaxle wants Bregan D'aerthe with a permanent Dock Ward presence; method: be indispensable to whoever resolves the vault situation; this is the long play that outlasts Dragon Heist
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:60: **Key design note:** Jarlaxle is the only villain whose faction (Bregan D'aerthe) the PCs can join. This must be written directly into the campaign structure, not treated as an edge case. A party that joins Bregan D'aerthe inverts **Sea Maidens Faire** — the Faire becomes their base, and Jarlaxle sends them after another faction's Eye.

campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:214: ***Bregan D'Aerthe Observer.*** The drow woman at the Yawning Portal balcony table is the campaign's earliest Bregan D'Aerthe presence. She is never named in Scene 1 and the party is not meant to notice her unless they ask who is watching the well. She reappears in Arc B as a background presence — same table, different evenings — and is eventually identified as part of Jarlaxle's Waterdeep intelligence network monitoring the Undermountain entrance. Her placement in Scene 1 is a retroactive payoff: by the time the party is dealing with the Sea Maidens Faire in Arc H, they can look back at the Yawning Portal and understand that Jarlaxle has had eyes on them since their first night in the city.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:234: 3. **BD watcher identity.** **Ryvarra** — a drow woman posing as a Calimshan cloth merchant. Full profile written and added to Appendix A (Section 11, Bregan D'Aerthe). Named field observer in Jarlaxle's Waterdeep intelligence network.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:25: While the renovation runs, Waterdeep comes to them. Guild inspectors arrive with clipboards and expectations. Neighbors appear with gifts, concerns, and agendas. Each of the city's seven adventuring factions sends its own recruiter, targeting specific party members by alignment, race, and reputation, not the group as a whole. The same party might find a Harper paper bird on one windowsill, a Bregan D'aerthe surveillance trail developing behind a drow PC, and a Doom Raiders flying snake delivering a note to the party's most morally flexible member, all in the same week. The factions want specific people. The party has to decide what they want in return.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:27: Between the renovation costs, the guild fees, and the neighbor who keeps filing complaints about rats, the city shows the party exactly what it costs to put down roots. Before the tavern opens, an invitation from the Sea Maidens Faire draws them to the Field of Triumph, where a carnival master named Zardoz Zord watches from a private box and Meloon Wardragon leads the opposing team in the final round. The arc's emotional high point is the Grand Opening — a full house, a poltergeist who has extended professional courtesy to the new owners, and an Ammalia Cassalanter attending as a generous patron, curious and charming and already counting the days to Founders' Day. The Twin Parades follow not long after, where something mechanical walks in one of the processions without explanation.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:105: *The BD Observer (New Content).* The drow from Arc A Scene 1 — Jarlaxle's Yawning Portal field observer — is spotted near Trollskull Alley during the renovation period. **DC 14 Wisdom (Perception)** to recognize her from Scene 1; **DC 17 Intelligence (Investigation)** to identify her as Bregan D'aerthe. She is professional and evasive if approached. Available only to parties with drow PCs or characters who made the Scene 1 Perception check. Her presence seeds the BD surveillance that escalates into Scene 4 recruitment. Cross-ref **Appendix A** (Ryvarra profile, BD Section).

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:129: *Bregan D'aerthe Recruitment Detail.* Drow PCs notice the surveillance over several days (passive Perception 18 spots glimpses; DC 15 Wisdom (Insight) confirms the watchers are specifically interested in drow party members). If the party reports the surveillance to the City Watch, Jarlaxle ends contact for now. If the party confronts the drow spies directly, they melt away and leave a single black eye patch on the cobblestones. The next morning, a haberdasher named J.B. Nevercott knocks at the manor's front door and maintains the fiction of a hat merchant for as long as it amuses him. **The full First Meeting scene — including J.B. Nevercott's read-aloud text and the moment the fiction drops — is in Appendix C.** Remix Option: BD operatives from campaign start — see **Appendix C (Bregan D'aerthe)** for the campaign-start variant.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:131: *Faction Renovation Assistance.* Once contact is established with a faction, assistance with the 1,250 gp renovation cost becomes available. Cross-ref **Appendix F, Faction Renovation Assistance** for full terms: Mirt's personal loan (500 gp, repayable as a favor), Jalester's civic grant (200 gp), the Enclave's *Fabricate* casting (−250 gp off renovation cost), Savra's guild connections (faster scheduling, favorable rates), Istrid Horn's Black Network loan (400 gp, party-set repayment), Jarlaxle's full bankrolling (up to 1,250 gp, repayable in operational access), Vajra's Tiny Hut and vault scrolls. Each offer has strings; the party's financing choices are their first real faction commitments.

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:161: *The Tournament Invitation.* An invitation arrives from the Sea Maidens Faire, a carnival flotilla docked in Waterdeep Harbor. Zardoz Zord, the Faire's flamboyant Illuskan captain, has reserved gladiatorial entry slots at the Field of Triumph and wants sponsored combatants. Entry is free; the prize is 50 gp per combatant plus whatever reputation accrues from public performance. The invitation is addressed to the party by name.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:195: *Jarlaxle Watching.* Zardoz Zord watches every bout from the Faire's reserved box, expansive and theatrically delighted by the party's performance. A **DC 18 Wisdom (Insight)** check reads the delight as genuine but not uncontrolled — something considerably more precise underneath the showmanship, the focused patience of a man evaluating something very carefully. He does not initiate contact beyond post-tournament congratulations. He is noting the party for Arc H.

campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:13: Dalakhar, a rock gnome spy working for exiled Lord Dagult Neverember, had been carrying the *Stone of Golorr* toward Trollskull Manor when four separate factions converged on the same alley at the same moment. Urstul Floxin's Zhentarim moved to intercept him from the north. The Gralhund nimblewright — a Lantanese construct sold by Jarlaxle Baenre's Sea Maidens Faire, armed with a *necklace of fireballs* by Lady Yalah Gralhund — watched from a rooftop, under orders to secure the Stone before any other faction could claim it. When Floxin appeared about to take Dalakhar alive, the nimblewright hurled a bead into the crowd. The fireball killed Dalakhar, two Zhentarim sellswords, eight bystanders, and the DM's chosen Arc B victim. Floxin survived — burned and furious — the Stone already gone. The nimblewright dropped from the roof, rifled Dalakhar's body, pocketed the Stone, and fled east across the rooftops toward Gralhund Villa. Floxin followed it back to the estate. Lady Gralhund disarmed him at swordpoint, confined him in a ground-floor room, and refused to yield the Stone. He killed his guards, escaped without it, and is now assembling a strike team for a forced return. That return is Arc D. The investigation that begins in Arc C must reach Gralhund Villa before the night of Ches 24th, when Floxin's team arrives and the situation becomes unrecoverable.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:152: | 9 | Faction member's nimblewright | Any ward | Brief uncomfortable moment; cleared quickly; incidentally surfaces that Jarlaxle sold nimblewrights across the city. |
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:162: **Purpose:** Connect the nimblewright investigation to Jarlaxle's operation. Frame the Dock Ward visit as a deliberate investigative redirect — it feels like a stall but introduces Jarlaxle, establishes the nimblewright network, provides three independent paths to the Gralhund connection, and seeds the Arc H heist target.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:168: *Captain Zord's Office.* Captain Zardoz Zord keeps regular hours and is known to enjoy interesting visitors. A party that attended the Field of Triumph (Arc B Scene 5) recognizes him from the private box. His real name is Jarlaxle Baenre. He does not advertise this. He already knows about the fireball, the gnome, and the Stone. He is deciding how much to share and at what price.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:172: - **Path 1: Ask Zord (Easy, Tips Off Jarlaxle).** Zord excuses himself briefly and returns with the Gralhund name, address, and the note that their nimblewright had "combat programming — unusual for a household model." He provides this freely. The consequence: the party has told Jarlaxle Baenre everything he needs to know. He deploys Bregan D'Aerthe agents to Gralhund Villa before the party arrives. Cross-ref Arc D (Jarlaxle's pre-positioning).
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:173: - **Path 2: Steal Jarlaxle's Records (Difficult, Preserves Secrecy).** Mini-heist aboard the *Eyecatcher* or the *Heartbreaker.* DC 16 Thieves' Tools (locked stateroom); DC 14 Stealth (nimblewright valet moving through the corridor); DC 15 Investigation (*Heartbreaker*) or DC 18 Investigation (*Eyecatcher*) to locate the ledger. Consequence: Jarlaxle discovers the theft within 24 hours but cannot identify who. He does not deploy to Gralhund Villa.
