The **complete ledger** (aboard the *Eyecatcher* or given directly by Jarlaxle to BD operatives) holds all nine entries:
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:198: **For Bregan D'Aerthe Characters. (New Content)** A BD character is escorted directly to Jarlaxle's private cabin. The persona drops — hat off, quieter smile, plain speech. He offers the complete ledger as a gift and a test. His ask: be in the room when the Stone conversation happens. **Renown: +1 for sharing intelligence back; +2 for withholding** — Jarlaxle is genuinely delighted to be outmaneuvered by his own people.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:202: **Tone:** Layered, transactional. Every conversation is a negotiation. Information has a price. Every person who helps the party is also pursuing their own agenda. Jarlaxle's warmth and the party's frustration at the apparent stall are both genuine — neither cancels the other.

campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:388: ## Jarlaxle's Conditional Activation
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:390: ***Investigation Quality Has Consequences.*** If the party reaches the Sea Maidens Faire, Jarlaxle enters the Grand Game as an active participant — he positions BD agents at Gralhund Villa, makes a direct approach, and begins treating the party as players worth engaging. If the party bypasses the Faire entirely and goes directly to the North Ward, he remains a background presence: aware, watching, not deployed. This is not a punishment for skipping Scene 4. It is a realistic consequence of investigative depth. The party that turns over more stones gives Jarlaxle more surface to react to.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:392: ***The Dock Ward as Deliberate Redirect.*** The Faire visit feels like a stall because it is one — the Gralhund nimblewright is not there. This is intentional. The frustration of following a real lead to a productive-but-incomplete result is exactly the kind of experience good investigative fiction creates. The payoff arrives the moment the party moves north: the detector's behavior changes on Saerdoun Street in a way that makes the Faire visit retroactively coherent. The intelligence gathered there — the ledger, the Cassalanter entry, the BD private meeting, the Jarlaxle identification — makes the detour worthwhile in ways the party cannot fully assess until later.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:13: The investigation that began in Trollskull Alley ends at the gate of Gralhund Villa on Saerdoun Street, North Ward. Urstul Floxin's Zhentarim strike team is coming on the night of Ches 24th. Xanathar's gazers are already watching the perimeter. Jarlaxle's Bregan D'Aerthe — if the party found their way to him in Arc C — has a team in the house across the street and knows more about what is inside than anyone. Lady Yalah Gralhund has the Stone and is holding it against all of them. The party's window is less than a day.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:49: *Jarlaxle Baenre / Captain Zardoz Zord (Bregan D'Aerthe — conditional on Arc C ev-04: Jarlaxle Informed).* Jarlaxle reaches out via a carnival acrobat who delivers a sealed note to wherever the party is staying. The note offers a meeting. In the meeting, he provides the complete nimblewright ledger, a detailed layout of the villa's upper floor from recordings made by the nimblewright's embedded clairvoyance crystal, and specific positions for Xanathar's four gazers on the perimeter. He does not mention that Fel'rekt Lafeen's team is already in the house across the street. His generosity is genuine in content. It is calculated in purpose. He is measuring whether the party can be trusted with real intelligence — and whether they are the kind of people who notice what was not offered. Cross-ref Arc C Design Note on "investigation quality has consequences."
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:53: **Tone:** Deliberative, each voice distinct. Mirt is warm and careful. Davil is blunt without being harsh. Jalester is genuinely cautious, not obstructive. Vajra is commandingly direct. Jarlaxle is warm and deflects every wrong question before it lands. Renaer is personal where the others are political. The party should feel the weight of six different agendas converging on the same question — and register that all six are nudging them toward the same answer, each for their own reasons.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:71: **Zhentarim Watchers (DC 12 Perception).** Two Zhent thugs on nearby rooftops, one black flying snake courier between them. They are watching the villa to ensure no one leaves before Floxin's team arrives. They noticed one of Xanathar's gazers several days ago but could not identify it. They are unaware of Jarlaxle's team in Artheyn Manor. If they spot the party approaching: the flying snake carries word to Floxin. His strike team mobilizes and arrives in 10 + 2d6 minutes. The party has that window.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:75: **Jarlaxle's Team at Artheyn Manor (DC 14 Perception — conditional on Arc C ev-04: Jarlaxle Informed).** If the party alerted Jarlaxle during Arc C — specifically setting the Jarlaxle Informed attunement in ev-04 — Fel'rekt Lafeen's team is positioned in Artheyn Manor, directly across Saerdoun Street. The owner (Radford Artheyn) has relocated to the Moonshae Isles and the manor is empty. Ground floor: Fel'rekt Lafeen and two drow at a front-room watch post. Top floor: two drow gunslingers and six additional drow with a sightline to the villa's upper windows. *A Directive from Jarlaxle* sits on a side table in the ground-floor room — recoverable after the raid if Artheyn Manor is searched. Jarlaxle's team has full situational awareness of both other factions' watchers. They are waiting for an opportunity, not a trigger. Cross-ref Appendix E for the directive document listing.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:77: If this condition is not met — the party bypassed the Sea Maidens Faire entirely, or entered it and set Ledger Stolen or Crystal Ball Accessed instead of Jarlaxle Informed — Artheyn Manor is empty and the fourth faction is absent from the confrontation.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:96: **Not knowable from outside:** Interior ceiling heights (20 feet throughout), specific room layouts, number and position of interior guards. These require entry, prior intelligence (Jarlaxle's layout if the party met him in Arc C), or a divination spell.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:104: - **Jarlaxle's Nimblewright Override (Optional Twist):** If Jarlaxle's team is present and the nimblewright becomes a combat liability, his drow can remotely override the construct via its embedded crystal, briefly seizing motor control. The nimblewright drops to its knees for one round (incapacitated), then shakes free. Usable once. The party should not know this happened.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:148: *Faction Reactive Behaviors (Daytime).* If the party is spotted by the Zhentarim watchers before entering: flying snake to Floxin, 10 + 2d6 minutes before his team mobilizes. The daytime infiltration becomes a race against Floxin's arrival. If the gazers track the party inside: they take up positions outside the walls and wait. They will not enter the estate. Jarlaxle's team (if present) watches; if the party is extracting the Stone before nightfall, Jarlaxle's team may attempt to intercept them on exit — not aggressively, but opportunistically.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:245: *Jarlaxle's Team in Motion (Conditional).* If Bregan D'Aerthe is present in Artheyn Manor, this sequence plays out concurrent with beats 8–10 above, once the raid is fully committed:
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:247: 1. Jarlaxle's drow gunslingers and two additional drow cross the rooftops and take up covering positions at the exterior of G13 — sightlines into the upstairs corridor.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:255: **BD-Affiliated PCs:** If any party member belongs to Bregan D'Aerthe, Jarlaxle's gunslingers extend their covering fire to include that character during the raid — sniping threats the PC is engaged with through the upper-floor windows. Fel'rekt acknowledges the PC with a nod when their paths cross. He does not break operational discipline to coordinate openly. The sharpshooter support is a quiet gift, not a joint operation.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:257: *Zhentarim Failure Is Not Foreordained.* If the party draws off or eliminates the Barracks reinforcements (beat 9), Floxin's team may actually win the ground floor and hold it. If Jarlaxle's team enters early — triggered by PC action rather than the natural timeline — the sequence collapses into improvisation. Xanathar's gazers observe throughout and will not intervene inside the estate.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:344: - **Bregan D'Aerthe (conditional on Arc C ev-04: Jarlaxle Informed):** Word reaches the party through whatever channel Krebbyg established during Arc C: *"We saw what happened at Gralhund. We know which factions were there. We know who left with what. If the party is interested in a full account — faction by faction — the theater has a back room. Price is one conversation. Not a mission. A meeting."* Jarlaxle was on the rooftops. He watched every entrance and exit.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:380: *Transition to Arc E.* Whatever the party holds at arc's end, the next phase of the Grand Game is already in motion. The Eyes of the Stone — the three objects needed to fully activate it — are distributed across the four villain faction lairs. The post-crackdown landscape has shifted: Davil is in Watch custody, Tashlyn is reorganizing, and Jarlaxle is reassessing the party's usefulness. The faction outpost phase begins. Arc E opens with the party deciding which faction to probe first.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:388: 1. **Day and night are separate scenes.** Scene 3 covers the daytime state (household dynamics, alarm escalation, optional homebrew additions). Scene 4 covers the nighttime Zhentarim raid (Floxin's timeline, Jarlaxle's team in motion, the raid's live outcome). Both scenes reference the same keyed areas (G1–G19); Scene 4 describes the night adversary roster as changes from Scene 3's baseline.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:390: 2. **Optional homebrew daytime defenses are included, clearly flagged.** Scene 3 contains an "Optional Daytime Escalation" section covering animated armor, spell glyphs, guard mastiffs, the proto-beholder, and Jarlaxle's nimblewright override twist. The section is labeled optional and marked as homebrew throughout.

campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:423: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Manshoon's Weave motivation and blackmailed Masked Lords (Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk); *A Directive from Jarlaxle* document listing; *Gralhund Study of the Grand Game* document listing; Yellowspire section (Chirada, Second Eye, teleportal); Xanathar Guild response teams (Scene 5 gazer intercept option).
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:435: What breaks if the party doesn't get the Stone? The campaign continues, but the party loses their leverage. They arrive at every subsequent negotiation without the asset that makes them worth negotiating with. Jarlaxle, who would have dealt with them as equals, deals with them as petitioners. Whichever faction holds the Stone begins locating the vault within 2d6 days. The party is now racing against an active countdown rather than holding the controlling position. This is consequential, not catastrophic — but it changes the campaign's dramatic shape significantly.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:443: The daytime state's optional homebrew escalation exists because the nighttime state's chaos naturally absorbs damage from rival factions — Zhents and guards fight each other, Jarlaxle's drow thin the opposition, and the party can navigate through the fighting rather than generating all of it. The daytime state has none of that. If the DM judges the standard Gralhund household too thin for a level 4 party arriving in force, the optional additions provide an equivalent threat ceiling. They are genuinely optional — a strong party with good reconnaissance should be able to handle the standard roster.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:449: This arc follows that philosophy. Scenes 3 and 4 are not fights with predetermined outcomes. They are situations with multiple possible resolutions, each shaped by the party's decisions, the rival factions' behaviors, and the specific sequence of events on the night. The ten-beat raid timeline is a spine, not a script — the DM can drop the party into it at any point, and the party's actions can derail it entirely. Zhentarim failure is not foreordained. Jarlaxle's team arriving early changes the calculus. The party drawing guards away from the barricade at G15b might let Floxin succeed at his hostage-taking.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:453: ## Jarlaxle's Test
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:455: The intelligence Jarlaxle offers in Scene 1 is completely accurate. He has the ledger. He has the villa layout. He knows where Xanathar's gazers are positioned. He gives the party all of it, freely, without apparent agenda.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:457: The gap in the offer is what matters. He does not mention Fel'rekt's team in Artheyn Manor. He does not disclose that Bregan D'Aerthe is already in position and will move the moment the raid begins. His generosity is genuine — but it is a test. He is measuring whether the party is the kind of organization that notices what was not offered, and whether they are capable of acting on incomplete intelligence without making it someone else's fault later. Parties who go to the villa, find the drow team across the street, and don't blame Jarlaxle for the omission have passed without knowing they were evaluated.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:459: This connects directly to Arc C's design note on "investigation quality has consequences." The quality of the party's relationship with Jarlaxle — and how much he chooses to share with them in subsequent arcs — is indexed to how they handle exactly these moments. A party that returns to Jarlaxle and asks why he didn't mention the drow team gets a smile and a redirect. A party that doesn't mention it at all gets a warmer reception next time.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:471: If the chase fails — if the Stone reaches a Bregan D'Aerthe outpost, a Zhentarim safe house, or Xanathar's custody — the campaign continues, but the party enters Arc E negotiating from weakness rather than strength. Jarlaxle will deal with them. Xanathar will not (Xanathar never deals). Manshoon's cell will know the party is active and has failed. These are real consequences that reshape the social landscape of the subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is Arc E's problem, not Arc D's.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:485: The Watch's sweep removes Davil from play, puts Tashlyn in a provisional leadership role she has not asked for, and signals to every faction in the city that the Grand Game has become visible to law enforcement. Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's operations in the North Ward become more careful. Jarlaxle's team leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown — who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn — begin to define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:35: That calculation is no longer accurate. Neverember is in Neverwinter. The parties holding his Eyes have been in Waterdeep long enough to develop their own plans for whatever the Stone might reveal. Jarlaxle Baenre holds his Eye aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, where it serves as leverage and curiosity in equal measure. The Cassalanter family, working through a network of cult agents and infernal diviners, located a second Eye buried in the crypt of a noblewoman who died without heirs, and extracted it. Xanathar holds the last in Sylgar's fishbowl in the lair beneath Skullport. Zhentarim envoys stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter and carried it to Xanathar as a gift. Xanathar killed them, kept the stone, and has never learned what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:39: The Stone now sits with the party. The Watch crackdown following the Gralhund Villa incident has disrupted Manshoon's Brindul Alley operation, scattered the Zhentarim response teams, and landed Davil Starsong in Watch custody on circumstantial evidence. Xanathar's Guild is consolidating — pulling agents tighter, increasing surveillance. Jarlaxle watches the party with quiet interest and no urgency; he holds something they need, and he is in no hurry to discuss terms. The Cassalanters, undisturbed, continue their research. Below the city's peaceful surface, four factions know the Stone has surfaced. Three of them want it back.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:93: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member (recruited during **Trollskull Alley**). Jarlaxle is always the primary Bregan D'aerthe contact, appearing in one of his surface identities — J.B. Nevercott or another persona appropriate to the channel established since **Trollskull Alley**. At renown 3 or higher, he has already briefed the Seven Masks Theater door staff; the party can meet him there freely. At renown 0, the theater is a location on a list, not a standing invitation. His approach this early in the quest is characteristically oblique. He does not offer tactical intelligence on other factions. What he offers is an acknowledgment: "You've been busy since Gralhund. I've been watching. I know what you're looking for, and I know where some of it is." The terms he proposes — actual terms, not favors — are information exchange, to be negotiated when both sides know what the other has. His unstated position: he wants to know what the Stone revealed before offering anything of equivalent value.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:121: **Bregan D'aerthe:** The Seven Masks Theater is a functioning public theater in the Dock Ward. "Rongquan Mystere" is a name in theater district playbills. Any investigation into drow surface activity or Dock Ward entertainment leads here without a check.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:127: *Cross-Faction Spillover.* Xanathar and Manshoon are actively at war; each monitors the other's losses. Hitting a Xanathar outpost raises Manshoon's alert tier by 1, and vice versa — both factions assume the other is responsible before they conclude it was the party. Cassalanter imp surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire creates a reciprocal intelligence channel; Cassalanter losses alert Jarlaxle that something is moving against infernal-adjacent operations in the city. See **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for full inter-faction dynamics.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:185: *Intel Yield.* Nar'l's correspondence is the primary entry path to Xanathar's Lair via the guildsign trail. The smokepowder reference seeds Nar'l's double-agent status — he is also in contact with Jarlaxle, a fact that pays off in **Xanathar's Lair** and **Sea Maidens Faire** when the BD-Xanathar connection surfaces.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:211: *Intel Yield.* Manshoon's name (first use in the campaign). The pass-amulet from Avareen (route to Kolat Towers). Ott Steeltoes as a potential **Xanathar's Lair** contact. The *Directive to Zorbog* naming Fenerus Stormcastle — a cross-faction thread pointing toward Bregan D'aerthe (Fenerus is a BD lamplighter; Manshoon has identified him as a soft target).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:291: ### Scene 7: Bregan D'aerthe Outposts
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:317: **Escalation: Bregan D'aerthe moves to Suspicious (Tier 2) if the party searches the house without BD renown; no tier change if they visit openly with Jarlaxle's introduction.** If Fenerus has been kidnapped and the party visits an empty house, no escalation triggers — the kidnap is not traceable to the party from the BD perspective. **Founders' Day Clock: 1–3 days (longer if staking out for Krebbyg).**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:333: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Primary objective: the BD safehouse dressing room and its stored intelligence. Stage Manager's Office (P6): *A Letter from K to J* — Krebbyg writing to Jarlaxle — warning that Fenerus's address has become known to a third party and recommending a new message drop. This letter cross-references the Fenerus outpost and confirms the BD communication architecture.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:337: *Soluun's Murders.* A pattern emerges if the party investigates the *Bloodstained Playbill* further: seat numbers corresponding to elves and half-elves who attended the theater and subsequently disappeared. Soluun Xibrindas is stalking victims from the audience after performances. The pattern is discoverable through theater records (DC 14 Investigation) or through asking Malcolm, who witnessed it. This is not required for the main intelligence pipeline — but it is leverage. Jarlaxle is aware of Soluun's behavior and has not acted on it. "He's useful" is his entire position on the matter.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:339: *Step 5 — Getaway.* Two drow guards occupy the safehouse dressing room when it is actively in use (roughly one-in-three chance at any given time). Malcolm is non-hostile and does not interfere. If the drow guards escape, they carry word to the Faire within hours — Jarlaxle knows before the party reaches the door. **Escalation: Bregan D'aerthe moves to Alert (Tier 3) if the dressing room is searched without BD sanction.** Jarlaxle's response is measured — no immediate retaliation, but the next contact with him begins from a different position. BD renown penalties apply if the outpost is raided without established relationship. **Founders' Day Clock: 2–4 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:381: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe).* Always the primary BD contact, in disguise. If BD outposts were hit, he is calm — he expected this eventually, and he finds the party's methodology worth acknowledging. "I showed you the door. You picked the lock anyway. I respect that." If BD outposts were not hit and the party approaches him directly, he offers nothing unprompted but is available to discuss terms. The gap between what Jarlaxle says and what he knows is always present; the party should feel it.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:445: - the **Factions** guide pages — Quest Hooks for Harpers, Lords' Alliance, Doom Raiders, and Bregan D'aerthe. New hooks for Emerald Enclave, Order of the Gauntlet, and Force Grey fill the gaps identified in planning.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:446: - **Notable Figures** — Profiles for Malcolm Brizzenbright, Ott Steeltoes, Fenerus Stormcastle, Jarlaxle (as Rongquan Mystere / J.B. Nevercott), Krebbyg Masq'il'yr, Soluun Xibrindas, Tashlyn Yafeera, Melannor Fellbranch, and Savra Belabranta.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:485: ***Soluun's Murders as Leverage.*** This intelligence unlocks no door — it's an internal BD problem Jarlaxle is aware of and hasn't acted on. Including it creates a conversation the party can have with Jarlaxle about what he is actually willing to enforce within his own organization. "He's useful" reveals something about Jarlaxle that no amount of charming banter does.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:495: **Harpers.** This quest's Harper hook is an ongoing operative benefit rather than a single scene beat. Once the party reaches Renown 30+, Mirt can direct embedded Harper informants to provide advance intelligence against Xanathar and Manshoon outposts — shift rotations, guard counts, unusual personnel — delivered before each operation rather than discovered during it. A separate informant inside the Sea Maidens Faire reports that Jarlaxle has been asking about the party by description; Mirt passes this to Harper PCs as a quiet heads-up in the days before **Sea Maidens Faire** begins. These benefits are not surfaced in Scene 2's consultation; they activate through maintained renown and are delivered as one informant report at the start of each outpost operation the party undertakes in Scenes 4–7, once a Harper PC has Renown 30+. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Harpers** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:17: Jarlaxle placed Nar'l Xibrindas in the Guild in 1491 and has relied on him as a double agent for the year since. Through Nar'l's monthly reports, the Bregan D'aerthe have assembled a precise picture of the lair's layout — including the secret passage at X14 that bypasses the panopticus entirely and opens forty feet from the Sanctum door. Jarlaxle has been waiting for an opportunity to retrieve the Stone and its Eye. He has been in no hurry. He has always been certain the opportunity would come.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:43: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle offers two things: the location of the secret entrance at X1 (reached via guildsign from the sewer network), and Nar'l Xibrindas's name and position inside the lair — framed as proof of trust. At renown 1–2, he describes Nar'l as "a useful contact who values his own continued good health." At renown 3+, he confirms Nar'l's BD allegiance directly. He does not mention his own heist plan. Everything he says is accurate. Everything he says is shaped.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:105: If a party member is BD: Nar'l recognizes them as colleagues and cooperates fully — no hesitation, no wary reassessment. He shares the complete guard rotation, confirms Xanathar's location in X19 and the dream nullifier's purpose, and actively manages alarms rather than offering a single suppression. He also reveals the existence of X14 (the secret passage to X19) if the party has not already found it. The grell constraint still applies: he will not act against it regardless of orders, because the grell is Xanathar's mechanism, not Jarlaxle's. He will evacuate immediately if the smokepowder option is chosen. One additional consequence of BD membership: the Jarlaxle simultaneous heist does not fire. Jarlaxle knew the party was BD, trusted them to handle it, and is waiting for Nar'l's report rather than sending a competing team.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:149: **X12 — Ahmaergo's Quarters.** Organized with obsessive precision: fight records, tournament schedules, arena financial ledgers, a battered sparring sword from Baldur's Gate. On the desk: *Briefing for Xanathar Concerning the Grand Game*. The document covers the current state of the Stone competition — intelligence on the Cassalanters, the Bregan D'aerthe (incomplete), and a physical description of the party accurate enough to confirm they've been watched. Ahmaergo knows who the party is.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:169: **X35 — Nar'l's Office.** Sparse and organized by a mind that trusts nothing to memory when paper will do. The desk holds Guild operational reports and a coded communication log in Jarlaxle's cipher (DC 16 Intelligence to decode without the key). In a false-bottomed drawer (DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation)): the *Report on the Seven Masks Theater* — a summary of Jarlaxle's operational activity through the theater's ownership chain, its connection to the Sea Maidens Faire, and a partial decoding of Jarlaxle's Grand Game position. Nar'l has kept this from Xanathar for eleven months. Finding it is one of three clue paths to **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:200: **Stage 4 — Xanathar Engaged.** Trigger: Xanathar is directly threatened or Sylgar is disturbed. Response: Xanathar deploys eye rays without tactical prioritization; Ahmaergo abandons his team position and moves directly to X19; the lair's entire combat-capable population converges. The only exits are through them, past them, or under cover of the Jarlaxle distraction.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:208: **Purpose.** The heist's pressure release. What goes wrong, how it escalates, and how the party gets out — managed alongside Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist, which may be helping, competing, or both simultaneously.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:212: *Jarlaxle's Simultaneous Heist.*
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:214: This event fires if all three conditions are met: (1) Jarlaxle is active in the Grand Game (conditional on **Fireball!** Sea Maidens Faire investigation), (2) the party has not allied with Jarlaxle or joined Bregan D'aerthe, and (3) the party has not yet recovered Eye #3 in **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:216: Jarlaxle's team — himself, one BD lieutenant, and a response team — uses whichever entry method the party did not. His specific advantage: Nar'l's reports over the past year gave him knowledge of the secret passage at X14, which bypasses the panopticus entirely and opens forty feet from the Sanctum door.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:218: | Party State | Jarlaxle's Action | Result |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:222: | Allied with Nar'l | Nar'l tips Jarlaxle; BD team avoids X19 for 20 minutes | Party may discover this coordination from Nar'l's behavior |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:223: | Party in X19 when Jarlaxle arrives | Three-way: PCs, Jarlaxle, Xanathar (present unless X20 was used) | Jarlaxle immediately negotiates — he'd rather split benefit than fight. His offer: the Eye for a favor to be named later. He does not negotiate down from this |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:224: | Party has the Eye, escaping | Jarlaxle withdraws his team | Clean exit. But Jarlaxle knows what they took, and **Sea Maidens Faire**'s stakes are now shaped by this |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:226: The simultaneous heist is a pressure-release valve. If the party's heist collapses at Stage 3 or 4, Jarlaxle's distraction pulls heat and opens a window. If the heist fails entirely and Jarlaxle claims the Eye, the campaign does not end. It redirects. **Sea Maidens Faire** becomes a recovery operation with the Eye in Jarlaxle's possession. The party enters **Sea Maidens Faire** from behind, under pressure, with less leverage. This is a consequence.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:230: Once Stage 3 fires, the party has approximately 4 minutes before Ahmaergo's team reaches their reported location. Use this window to complete the extraction and reach an exit, destroy the dream nullifier to create a secondary distraction, coordinate with Jarlaxle's team if encountered, or detonate the smokepowder option (if prepared in Scene 3). The cascade timing is exact and pre-specified; the GM does not need to improvise escalation decisions.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:240: **Tone.** Chaos, managed. Everything the party planned for is tested simultaneously. Jarlaxle may be helping, competing, or both. The cascade is advancing. The Eye may or may not be in hand. The scene is designed to feel overwhelming but not hopeless; every exit remains available if the party moves decisively.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:271: - The remaining Eye's location resolves fully. Which impression sharpens depends on which arc the party completed first. If **Cassalanter Villa** was first (already raided), the final impression is: "A ship in the harbor. The Scarlet Marpenoth. A bald elf with a year of patience." If **Sea Maidens Faire** was first (Jarlaxle's Eye already recovered), the final impression is: "A villa in the Sea Ward. A temple beneath it, stone floor engraved with the Cassalanter house mark. The children's laughter has a hollow quality." The Stone identifies each Eye by proximity-sense, not by name.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:290: **Bregan D'aerthe.** If Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist fired: he knows the outcome. He will not contact the party immediately; he processes before communicating. Either way, **Sea Maidens Faire**'s stakes are now shaped by this quest's result.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:300: - **Jarlaxle (if contacted):** Characteristically oblique. "I hear there was excitement near Skullport last night." If pressed on whether he was there: "I am always where I'm needed." He never confirms. He never denies.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:343: - **Sea Maidens Faire** — Stone 1-Eye impressions (ship in harbor, dark-skinned elf) and Report on Seven Masks Theater (X35) both point toward **Sea Maidens Faire**. Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist outcome shapes **Sea Maidens Faire** stakes directly.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:384: ## Jarlaxle's Simultaneous Heist
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:388: First, dramatic tension: the party should not be the only competent actor in Waterdeep. Jarlaxle has better lair intelligence than the party; he has had a spy embedded for a year. The heist's outcome is not guaranteed to favor the party simply because they are the protagonists.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:390: Second, failure-forward design: a heist that can fail completely needs a designed resolution that keeps the campaign moving. Jarlaxle claiming the Eye redirects the story to **Sea Maidens Faire** as a recovery operation rather than ending it. The party enters **Sea Maidens Faire** from behind, with less leverage, under pressure. This is a consequence.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:392: Third, **Sea Maidens Faire** setup: whether Jarlaxle got the Eye or didn't, **Sea Maidens Faire**'s stakes are directly shaped by this quest's outcome. A party that beat Jarlaxle to the Eye negotiates from strength. A party that didn't negotiates from weakness. **Sea Maidens Faire** plays differently depending on which way this went, which means this quest's outcome creates meaningful forward variation.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:394: The three trigger conditions have been carefully set to ensure the heist fires only when it creates the right dramatic conditions. If the party has allied with Jarlaxle or is BD members themselves, running his team against them is wrong. If **Sea Maidens Faire** is already complete, the Eye situation is resolved and the heist is moot.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:47: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle has been watching the Cassalanters through an embedded source he does not name (Vessa, his doppelganger in the Cassalanter social circle), and he knows what the family has done. He keeps it from Bregan D'aerthe members unless they work it out themselves. He considers them "the most dangerous faction in the Grand Game because they have something none of the others have: a deadline." He will not explain what the deadline is for. He can add, if pressed, that the Cassalanters need the vault gold urgently — not from ambition, but from a fixed date on a calendar. If the party has already found evidence of Cassalanter operations through **Faction Outposts**, Jarlaxle confirms what they have rather than leading with it: "Then you've seen the edges of it. The center is underneath the villa." At renown 3+ and only with that prior evidence in hand, he provides his operative's observations on the villa's schedule and staff patterns — and adds, obliquely: "They intend to solve a problem with other people's lives. One hundred invitations. Ninety-nine cups of wine. A calculation dressed as charity." He will not explain the calculation. He does not mention "Vessa."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:133: **"Vessa" (Lymeria Lhaurilstar).** During social events, a doppelganger posing as the noblewoman "Vessa" circulates in C15 (the banquet hall). She is a Bregan D'aerthe operative planted to monitor the Cassalanters. She knows the villa's public layout, the approximate location of the cult's gathering space ("below the wine cellar"), and that the Cassalanters have something the Grand Game factions want. If the party has BD renown 1+ (Initiate rank — any BD member), she reveals herself on approach — a brief flash of her true features, a knowing look. Otherwise, she is a mysterious ally who seems to know too much and asks pointed questions about why the party is really here. She provides: distraction (a fainting spell, a spilled drink, a loud argument with another guest), cover stories ("They're with me — the Lhauri family's representatives"), and passage through the banquet areas without social exposure checks. She will not enter the temple.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:334: - The remaining Eye's location resolves fully. Which impression sharpens depends on which quest the party completed first. If **Xanathar's Lair** was first (Xanathar's lair already raided), the final impression is: "A ship in the harbor. The Scarlet Marpenoth. A bald elf with a year of patience." If **Sea Maidens Faire** was first (Jarlaxle's Eye already recovered), the final impression is: "Beneath Skullport. An eye, unblinking in carved stone, wet from underground springs. It has been waiting longer than anyone has been looking." The Stone identifies each Eye by proximity-sense, not by name.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:359: **Bregan D'aerthe.** "Vessa" reports the outcome to Jarlaxle. If the party recovered the Eye, Jarlaxle adjusts his plan for Eye #3 — he knows they are two-thirds of the way to the Vault.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:373: - *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe).* Genuinely moved — the closest thing to unguarded emotion the party has seen from him. The children are saved. He recovers quickly: "Well. One fewer rival faction. How convenient."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:380: - *Jarlaxle.* Amused. "You gave away five hundred thousand gold dragons to save two children. I have never met anyone so wealthy in compassion and so poor in business sense."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:393: - *Jarlaxle.* Offers, obliquely, to "look into certain infernal matters" — hinting that Bregan D'aerthe might have resources to contest the soul-claim. He does not promise success. This is a genuine lifeline or a characterful dead end, depending on how the GM runs **Vault of Dragons**.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:436: - **Sea Maidens Faire** — Stone 2-Eye impressions (ship in harbor, Scarlet Marpenoth, bald elf) point toward **Sea Maidens Faire**. "Vessa" the BD operative in this quest is a preview of Jarlaxle's intelligence operations.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:473: The parallel heist mirrors Jarlaxle's simultaneous operation in **Xanathar's Lair**, with three structural functions.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:9: Jarlaxle Baenre had been in Waterdeep since Marpenoth of 1491. His Sea Maidens Faire sailed into the harbor on Ches 21, at the start of the last tenday of Ches, when the Fleetswake festival filled the harbor with color and noise and the city looked the other way at almost anything. Three ships: the Heartbreaker and Hellraiser, galleons painted crimson and gold that docked on the Mistshore pier, their hulls flanking a festive walkway strung with lanterns; and the Eyecatcher, the flagship, anchored a quarter-mile out in Deepwater Harbor where it glittered in the morning light and refused to be docked like ordinary ships. Between the Heartbreaker and the Hellraiser, the Sea Maidens Faire drew crowds — fire-eaters, acrobats, fortune-tellers, caged beasts, nimblewrights performing as carnival attractions. It was an excellent show. It was designed to be.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:11: The Scarlet Marpenoth had lain moored in the harbor all year, and the night the fleet arrived it took its place beneath the Eyecatcher. Attached to the Eyecatcher's keel, hidden beneath twenty feet of harbor water, it had been running silent since. The submarine was gnome-engineered, cramped, and extraordinarily well-maintained — crewed by three gnome engineers who treated it with the devotion of master artificers and the paranoia of men who knew exactly what the vessel contained. Jarlaxle used it as his command center, his vault, and his escape plan. He slept in the submarine. The Eye of the Stone of Golorr sat in a velvet-lined compartment built into the base of the crystal ball pedestal that controlled his nimblewright surveillance network across Waterdeep. He had placed it there deliberately — close to the thing he used most, in a compartment invisible to anyone who didn't know to look. Jarlaxle was patient. The Eye would keep.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:13: The nimblewright network was Jarlaxle's primary intelligence asset in Waterdeep. Nine nimblewrights sold to nine wealthy households at 25,000 gold pieces each — a steal, and deliberately so. Each nimblewright contained a paired clairvoyance crystal feeding recorded surveillance to the master crystal ball in U4. The network gave him eyes inside the homes of nobles, guild leaders, and civic officials. He knew about affairs and debts and crimes and alliances that their participants assumed were secret. He intended to use this knowledge to build the political case for Luskan's admission to the Lords' Alliance — the campaign that was, underneath every disguise, the reason he had come to Waterdeep at all. Five hundred thousand stolen dragons, recovered from the Vault of Dragons and deployed as a diplomatic investment, would help. The network would help. Having the Stone of Golorr in cooperative hands would help most of all.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:15: The Eye had reached him the night before Ches 1, through Bregan D'aerthe intelligence work — the same network that had placed Nar'l Xibrindas inside Xanathar's Guild a year earlier, in 1491. Jarlaxle understood the Eye's significance better than any other faction in the Grand Game, which is why he was not rushing. He would watch. He would learn who else was moving on the Stone. If the party pursuing the Stone proved competent, he would engage them on his own terms. If they did not, he would take the Eye himself when the moment was right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:25: **Purpose.** Translate **Faction Outposts** outpost intelligence and prior Jarlaxle interactions into actionable fleet knowledge. Each faction contact provides ship-specific intelligence calibrated to what the party already discovered. Same format as **Xanathar's Lair** Scene 1 and **Cassalanter Villa** Scene 1: a montage of faction consultations, each adding a piece to the picture.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:33: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* Mirt knows the Sea Maidens Faire is not what it appears. Harper agents have observed that crew members on the Eyecatcher avoid direct sunlight and that the carnival's operating hours do not match a legitimate entertainment business. If BD outposts were hit in **Faction Outposts**, Mirt confirms the drow connection: "The carnival is a front. The crew are drow in disguise. The question is what the front is covering." He does not know about the Scarlet Marpenoth unless the party reported Zelifarn's observation through Force Grey. If they did, his assessment sharpens: "An underwater vessel attached to a carnival ship in Waterdeep's harbor. That is not entertainment infrastructure." Mirt can provide harbor patrol schedules and identify which Watch officers have been paid to look away from the Faire's private docking arrangements. At renown 10+, Mirt can activate a Harper informant embedded in the Faire's carnival staff for one specific intelligence request — crew shift rotation, Jarlaxle's schedule for a particular date, or the location of a keyed area not visible from the pier (DC 13 Charisma check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two weeks).
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:35: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* The Sea Maidens Faire uses private docking arrangements that circumvent standard Watch inspection protocols — Jalester flagged this irregularity in **Fireball!**. He can provide the Faire's official docking permits, which list crew numbers that do not match observed headcounts. If BD outposts were hit, Jalester can arrange harbor patrols to be "elsewhere" during a specific window. He also notes that the Council of Musicians filed an objection to the Faire as a public entertainment, resolved through the Jesters' Guild — the Faire has legal protection that makes Watch intervention politically difficult. If the alliance path is available (party has faction contacts willing to sponsor Luskan's Alliance bid), Jalester is the most relevant contact: any political commitment regarding Luskan runs through the Alliance council, and he will want to be briefed before the meeting with Jarlaxle. At renown 10+, Jalester can arrange a formal harbor inspection by Watch authority — a 90-minute window when three Faire crew members must answer questions aboard the Heartbreaker, reducing the Eyecatcher's available response roster. Requires 24 hours' notice; usable once.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:41: *Vajra Safahr (Force Grey — renown 1+).* Arcane assessment of the fleet. Vajra detects transmutation and illusion magic saturating the Eyecatcher — consistent with sustained disguise effects on multiple humanoids. She identifies the nimblewright crystal ball's signature as a persistent clairvoyance network: "Someone on that ship is watching nine locations in this city simultaneously through paired crystals." If the party completed Force Grey Mission 2 (Zelifarn and the Harbor), Vajra has already filed the submarine observation in her threat assessment. She now provides the critical operational detail: the Scarlet Marpenoth detaches from the Eyecatcher's hull via a keel collar mechanism. If Jarlaxle decides to flee, the submarine separates and submerges independently. "If your target can leave underwater, you need a plan for that." At renown 10+, Vajra stations a Blackstaff mage in a harbor skiff with a communication bead matched to one in the party's possession; once per contact, the mage casts silence or fog cloud at range, provided the party is within 200 feet of the harbor entrance.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:43: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* The Doom Raiders have had dealings with Jarlaxle's operation — he approached them early in the campaign about intelligence sharing, which they declined. Tashlyn or Davil can provide the Faire's supply schedule: food deliveries from the Butchers' Guild on Ches 22, 26, and Tarsakh 2, 6; water cask deliveries from the Watermen's Guild every three days; exotic animal feed from a private supplier in the Field Ward. Each delivery requires a crew of 4–6 and takes 45 minutes. Delivery crews have unescorted access to the cargo holds. The Doom Raiders also note that the Faire's revenue does not match its operating costs: "They're spending more than they're earning. The carnival is subsidized by something else." At renown 10+, Yagra Stonefist accompanies the supply delivery infiltration — she knows enough Guild cant to bluff past a cargo inspection and can carry concealed equipment for the party without triggering search protocols.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:45: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle contacts them directly through the existing relationship. His pitch is straightforward: he knows the party has the Stone of Golorr, and possibly an Eye or two already restored. He wants the hoard — not for himself, but for Luskan. "Five hundred thousand dragons buys a seat on the Lords' Alliance council that Luskan cannot earn by reputation alone. Let me tell you why that matters, and what I can offer in return." He lays out Luskan's Alliance bid plainly. He does not threaten. He frames cooperation as mutual benefit. How much he reveals beyond the pitch depends on trust: at Operative rank (Renown 10+), Jarlaxle deals plainly and may offer Eye #3 outright as a show of good faith. At Soldier or Initiate rank, he wraps the same agenda in the Zardoz Betrayal Pitch — a competence and loyalty test before he shows his full hand (see Scene 3). If no PC is a BD member, Jarlaxle is not available as a briefing contact.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:57: *Identifying the Score.* Eye #3 is aboard the Scarlet Marpenoth, in Jarlaxle's stateroom (U4), sitting in a velvet-lined compartment built into the base of the crystal ball pedestal. The Stone's impression (if 1+ Eyes restored) sharpens to: "a ship in the harbor, the Scarlet Marpenoth, a bald elf with a year of patience." **Faction Outposts** documents — Fenerus Stormcastle's decoded messages confirming intelligence flows to the Sea Maidens Faire, Malcolm Brizzenbright's ghost testimony that "Rongquan Mystere" met with drow who mentioned the Faire — converge on the fleet. **Xanathar's Lair** documents — the Report on Seven Masks Theater from X35 — provide a third confirmation path.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:71: **Eyecatcher (anchored in deep harbor).** The flagship. A quarter-mile from the pier, reachable by harbor skiff (departing hourly from the pier, 2 sp), swimming (DC 12 Athletics, cold water, awkward current), or Zelifarn's underwater transport. Boarding from a skiff requires a credible reason (DC 14 Deception) or an invitation from Zord. Boarding clandestinely at night: DC 14 Stealth to reach the hull, DC 16 to board unseen. The Eyecatcher contains Jarlaxle's public quarters (J30), crew quarters, the nimblewright storage (J18), and — critically — the trapdoor in J30 that accesses the Scarlet Marpenoth beneath.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:73: **Scarlet Marpenoth (submarine, attached beneath Eyecatcher).** Two access points: the trapdoor in J30 (Zardoz Zord's cabin), which requires reaching the Eyecatcher's aft deck and entering the locked cabin; and the external hull access, reachable underwater from the Eyecatcher's keel — a hatch visible from beneath (DC 14 Perception from underwater, or Zelifarn's guidance). The submarine is the score's location. U4 (Jarlaxle's stateroom) holds the crystal ball on its pedestal, with Eye #3 in the compartment at the pedestal's base.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:79: **Shipwright's Ball (Ches 25).** Jarlaxle attends the Ball in disguise — as J.B. Nevercott or Rongquan Mystere. The Eyecatcher is reduced to skeleton crew with no captain aboard. Social cover for surveillance: half the party can attend the Ball while the other half boards the Eyecatcher. Duration: full evening (6 hours). This is the best diplomatic window — Jarlaxle is accessible in a social setting where business is expected.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:99: **Path 1: Heist Against Jarlaxle (standard).** The party treats Jarlaxle as an adversary. They infiltrate the ships, navigate to the Scarlet Marpenoth, and steal Eye #3 from U4. This is the default path and the one most of the scene structure supports. The heist plays out in Scenes 4 and 5.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:101: **Path 2: Alliance with Jarlaxle (diplomatic).** The party negotiates directly. Jarlaxle's primary goal is Luskan's admission to the Lords' Alliance, not Eye #3. If the party can credibly promise political support through faction contacts — Mirt influences Harper votes on the Alliance council, Vajra has Laeral Silverhand's ear, Jalester represents the Alliance directly — Jarlaxle will trade Eye #3. His ask: "Luskan deserves a seat at the table. I need someone at the council who can make that argument." The political cost is real: Luskan has a terrible reputation (piracy, drow influence, historical slavery), and sponsoring its admission strains every faction relationship the party has built. This path bypasses Scenes 4 and 5 entirely. A DC 16 Charisma (Persuasion) check with advantage if the party presents concrete commitments from two or more faction contacts. Jarlaxle's Insight is +11; he detects empty promises instantly.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:103: **Path 3: Jarlaxle as Patron (BD-member PCs).** If a PC joined Bregan D'aerthe during **Trollskull Alley**, Jarlaxle may give them the Eye as a reward for proven service (Operative rank, Renown 10+), or use the Zardoz Betrayal Pitch as a loyalty and competence test (Initiate or Soldier rank, Renown 1–9). This path is conditional on an active BD membership. It bypasses the heist but carries its own stakes: the PC's factional loyalty is being tested, and the report back to Jarlaxle determines whether BD treats the party as assets or liabilities going forward.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:105: *The Zardoz Betrayal Pitch (conditional).* This fires if: a PC has BD membership at Initiate or Soldier rank (Renown 1–9) and has not yet identified Jarlaxle as the heist target, OR Jarlaxle has independent reason to be interested in the party from prior interactions — **Fireball!**'s Sea Maidens Faire visit, the nimblewright investigation, faction activity he has observed through the surveillance network. Jarlaxle creates contact through an intermediary (J.B. Nevercott approaching them at a tavern, a note via Fenerus Stormcastle's network, an Eyecatcher dinner invitation from "Captain Zord"). If no PC is a BD member and the party has had no meaningful prior contact with the Faire, the Pitch does not fire — Jarlaxle has no channel and no occasion.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:107: Jarlaxle (as J.B. Nevercott) hires the party to rob "Captain Zardoz Zord." He provides a cover story: Zord has stolen a valuable artifact from Nevercott's employer; the party is to board the Eyecatcher, locate the artifact in the captain's quarters, and deliver it to Nevercott. He provides floor plans (accurate), crew schedules (accurate), and a fee of 500 gp, paid in advance. Everything he provides is real. The job is real. The deception is in who benefits.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:109: Three purposes: testing the party's operational competence (can they actually pull off a ship heist?); testing their loyalty (will they report back honestly about what they found, or keep the Eye?); demonstrating that Jarlaxle is always three steps ahead (when the party reports back, he drops the Nevercott disguise and explains). If the party completed the mission and reported honestly, Jarlaxle is impressed and moves directly to the alliance path. If they completed it and kept the Eye, Jarlaxle is disappointed but adjusts — he respects competence even when it is directed against him. If they saw through the deception mid-mission and confronted him, Jarlaxle is delighted: "You're smarter than most. Good."
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:117: Zelifarn also introduces this quest's moral dimension. The crystal ball in U4 — the Nimblewright Crystal Ball that controls Jarlaxle's surveillance network — belonged to Zelifarn's mother Asphosis. Jarlaxle's agents killed Asphosis and took it. Zelifarn does not know this when the party first engages him for assistance on this quest. He discovers it only if the party brings him to U4 or tells him what they found there. His reaction is grief, then cold fury, then a request: "That was hers. Bring it to me." The party must decide: the crystal ball controls nine paired clairvoyance crystals across Waterdeep. Returning it to Zelifarn destroys the surveillance network and honors a grieving son's claim. Keeping it gives the party a city-wide intelligence tool. The decision is not mechanically consequential for this quest's completion — the Eye is separate from the crystal ball — but it is morally consequential and shapes the party's relationship with Zelifarn for the remainder of the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:126: | Suspicious | Seven Masks Theater hit | Patrols increased on all ships. Jarlaxle aware someone is investigating BD. Carnival hours shortened. Drow operatives more visible on the Eyecatcher deck. |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:127: | Alert | Both BD outposts hit | Ships on high alert. 4 of 8 nimblewrights from J18 now active and patrolling. Jarlaxle may have relocated Eye #3 to his person. External submarine hatch sealed and trapped (DC 16 Thieves' Tools to disarm; 4d6 lightning damage on failure). |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:128: | Lockdown | Spillover + both outposts | Carnival shut down. Ships preparing early departure (Tarsakh 15). Jarlaxle relocated to the Scarlet Marpenoth permanently. Full combat roster. All 8 nimblewrights from J18 active. Pier access restricted to crew only. |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:140: *Running This Scene.* This quest's operation uses a three-beat improvisation structure rather than a staged alarm cascade. Something Goes Wrong → The Complication Escalates → Jarlaxle Confrontation or Clean Escape. This is deliberate: the caper genre does not use tightening pressure so much as rolling improvisation. When something goes wrong aboard the Eyecatcher, the party has seconds to respond — not minutes. The response creates the next complication.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:142: Jarlaxle's location by operation window: during carnival parades, he is marching in the parade (not aboard the Eyecatcher); during the Shipwright's Ball, he is ashore attending the Ball; during standard operating hours, he is in J32 (the sauna, his preferred location when aboard); during Zord's business errands in the city, he is away for 2–4 hours. The GM does not roll for Jarlaxle's position — the operation window determines it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:152: **J10 (Captain's Cabin — Upper Level).** The operational commander's office. 1 drow mage (in human disguise, serving as the Eyecatcher's captain — not Jarlaxle) + 1 nimblewright valet. The captain knows the trapdoor in J30 leads to the Scarlet Marpenoth but does not have the key. The nimblewright responds to intruders with combat programming. During the Shipwright's Ball: captain absent (attending with Jarlaxle), nimblewright present.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:156: **J18 (Storage).** 8 deactivated nimblewrights. These activate on a command word from the captain or Jarlaxle. At Unaware tier: all 8 dormant. At Suspicious tier: 2 active, patrolling the main deck. At Alert tier: 4 active, patrolling corridors. At Lockdown tier: all 8 active.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:158: **J29 (Guest Cabin).** Margo Verida (bard) + Khafeyta Murzan (swashbuckler). Jarlaxle's companions — skilled combatants and genuine friends. They can be charmed, deceived, or negotiated with: Margo responds to flattery about her music (DC 13 Persuasion), Khafeyta responds to a good fight story (DC 14 Persuasion). Neither will betray Jarlaxle, but both can be distracted.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:160: **J30 (Zardoz Zord's Cabin).** Jarlaxle's private quarters aboard the Eyecatcher. 1 nimblewright valet. Contains: the Nimblewright Sales Ledger (complete list of nine buyers — cross-reference **Fireball!** Scene 4 for the partial ledger the party may already hold); the Report on the Cultists of Asmodeus (identifies the Asmodean Shrine on Aveen Street and the ancient worship site below the Sea Ward — Three Clue Rule path to **Cassalanter Villa**, if not yet completed); Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (references all four factions' positions including Manshoon's Kolat Towers — Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**). The trapdoor to the Scarlet Marpenoth is beneath a rug in the aft corner — DC 16 Perception to locate, DC 18 Thieves' Tools to unlock or Jarlaxle's key.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:162: **J31 (Training Area).** 5 attack mannequins — training dummies that animate when someone not carrying a BD identification token enters the room. They fight as animated armor. A BD identification token (recoverable from any defeated drow crew member, or given to BD-affiliated PCs by Jarlaxle) suppresses the animation.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:164: **J32 (Jarlaxle's Sauna).** A steam room with exotic wood paneling and a mechanical ventilation system. This is Jarlaxle's location during standard operating hours. If the party encounters him here, he is unarmed and unarmored but wearing his hat of disguise (Zardoz Zord appearance). He is not defenseless — his bracer of flying daggers and ring of spell storing are always worn. He is relaxed, and an encounter here has a different texture than a confrontation on deck. He will talk first. He will talk for a long time if the conversation is interesting.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:168: **U3 (Soluun's Stateroom).** Soluun Xibrindas is present during standard operating hours and at night; absent during parade windows (he uses the crowds for stalking). Contains: a Letter from Nar'l Xibrindas confirming the BD mole in Xanathar's organization and mentioning the Seven Masks Theater purchase. Soluun is the most dangerous combatant aboard the submarine — volatile, cruel, and fanatically loyal to Jarlaxle. He attacks intruders without hesitation and without the non-lethal restraint other BD operatives show. If the party contains an elf or half-elf, Soluun fixates on them.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:170: **U4 (Jarlaxle's Stateroom).** The score. A crystal ball sits on a pedestal in the center of the stateroom — the Nimblewright Crystal Ball, connected to nine paired clairvoyance crystals across Waterdeep. Eye #3 is in a velvet-lined compartment built into the pedestal's base: DC 14 Investigation to locate the compartment; DC 13 Thieves' Tools to extract the Eye without disturbing the crystal ball's alignment. Disturbing the crystal ball without extracting the Eye first triggers a silent alarm to J10 — the captain dispatches the nimblewright valet and alerts Jarlaxle if reachable. The crystal ball is separate from the Eye — it can be taken, left, or given to Zelifarn as a separate decision.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:188: **Beat 2 — The Complication Escalates.** The initial improvisation creates a secondary problem. The distracted crew member told someone; the defeated nimblewright's absence is noticed; the bypassed lock was on a schedule. The escalation narrows options without closing them. Crew begin moving toward the disturbance. If Jarlaxle is aboard, he becomes aware that something is happening.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:190: **Beat 3 — Jarlaxle Confrontation or Clean Escape.** Either the party extracts before Jarlaxle intervenes (clean escape), or Jarlaxle confronts them (see Scene 5). There is no state between these two outcomes in the caper genre.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:198: **Purpose.** What goes wrong, what goes sideways, and how the party gets out — managed alongside the Manshoon parallel strike and the Jarlaxle confrontation.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:206: **Trigger Conditions.** The Manshoon strike always fires. On the heist path, the strike begins 30 minutes after the party boards the Eyecatcher. On the alliance or patron path, the strike hits while the party is aboard negotiating or receiving the Eye — Jarlaxle's crew repels the Zhentarim, but the attack gives Jarlaxle a reason to ask the party for payback: "They came to my ship. In my harbor. I want to know where they came from, and I want it to stop." This becomes a direct hook into **Kolat Towers** — Jarlaxle sends the party after Manshoon at Kolat Towers, providing whatever BD intelligence the network has on the location. The Zhent attack transforms the alliance and patron paths from clean resolutions into obligations with teeth.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:217: | PCs allied or patron path (not heisting) | Zhentarim board and attack BD crew | Jarlaxle's crew fights back; party can assist or stand clear. Jarlaxle uses the attack as leverage: "Now you owe me — find where they came from." Pass-amulets recovered from defeated Zhentarim + Jarlaxle's BD intelligence on Manshoon = strongest **Kolat Towers** entry path available |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:219: *Facing Jarlaxle.* If Jarlaxle confronts the party — because the alarm reached him, because the Zhentarim strike drew him out, or because he was in J30 or J32 when the party arrived:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:221: Jarlaxle does not attack first. He talks. He assesses. He asks why they are here and what they want. His first instinct is always to negotiate. If the plan was good and the improvisation creative — if the party got further than he expected — Jarlaxle is genuinely impressed. He may offer the Eye in exchange for a favor, information, or the political alliance from Path 2.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:223: If the party was sloppy, Jarlaxle is disappointed but not hostile. He offers a deal: leave the Eye, take whatever else they found, owe him nothing — or take the Eye and owe him a favor. He frames every outcome as a transaction.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:225: If the party was running the Zardoz Betrayal Pitch and reports honestly, Jarlaxle drops the disguise, reveals himself, and offers the Eye freely. The test is passed.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:227: If combat occurs, Jarlaxle uses hit-and-run tactics: smoke bombs (20-foot-radius darkness), levitation, illusions (mirror image, major image of himself running the wrong direction), and his bracer of flying daggers. He fights to escape, not to kill. If reduced to 30 HP, he disengages and retreats to the Scarlet Marpenoth. The submarine is his exit plan — if he reaches U7b, the gnomes begin submerging. The party has 3 minutes to escape or descend with the submarine, which resurfaces 2 miles offshore 30 minutes later. Soluun fights to kill; Fel'rekt and Krebbyg fight to disable and cover Jarlaxle's retreat.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:237: **Submarine surfacing (if the party controls U7b).** If the party neutralized the submarine crew and has a character with proficiency in water vehicles — or a gnome engineer willing to cooperate (DC 16 Persuasion) — the Scarlet Marpenoth can detach and surface independently. This is the most audacious extraction. It removes the submarine from Jarlaxle's control, which is a significant campaign consequence.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:239: **Tone.** The complications should feel like a heist going sideways — not catastrophic, but requiring rapid adaptation. Jarlaxle's confrontation should feel like a chess match, not a battle. The Manshoon strike adds genuine danger — the Zhentarim are not charming; they are violent, and their presence transforms the caper into something more dangerous. The contrast between Jarlaxle's wit and the Zhentarim's brutality is the scene's defining dynamic.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:270: **Bregan D'aerthe.** Set the "BD operational?" flag. Five states:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:272: - *Allied (diplomatic resolution):* Jarlaxle traded the Eye for a political commitment. BD is now a campaign ally providing intelligence, operational support, and Jarlaxle's considerable personal charm. The cost: the party owes Luskan's Alliance bid genuine support. Jarlaxle will collect. The alliance does not make BD harmless — it makes them a partner with their own agenda.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:273: - *Impressed (heist succeeded, Jarlaxle confronted and negotiated):* Jarlaxle lost the Eye but gained respect for the party. He is not hostile — he is intrigued. BD continues to operate but treats the party as worthy opponents rather than targets. Jarlaxle may approach them for **Kolat Towers** or **Vault of Dragons** cooperation.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:274: - *Robbed (heist succeeded, clean escape):* Jarlaxle lost the Eye without knowing who took it (possible at Unaware tier). BD launches an intelligence operation to identify the thieves. Jarlaxle is embarrassed, which makes him more decisive when he discovers the truth.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:275: - *Hostile (combat, casualties among BD lieutenants):* If the party killed Soluun, Fel'rekt, or Krebbyg, Jarlaxle's response escalates from professional to personal. He reaches for leverage before force, but he reaches harder. BD response teams become more aggressive in future encounters.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:276: - *Patron (BD member received the Eye):* Jarlaxle's investment in the PC is confirmed. BD renown increases. The PC is now a trusted operative, which means more missions, more intelligence, and more expectations.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:294: - *Jarlaxle (if allied or impressed):* If the party negotiated the alliance: "I trust you'll remember our arrangement. Luskan is patient. I am less so." If the party robbed him cleanly: silence for three days, then a note on Seven Masks Theater stationery — "Well played." If the party fought him: a more cautious approach through intermediaries, testing whether hostility has become permanent or was situational.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:298: All contacts shift to problem-solving mode. Where is the Eye now? Is Jarlaxle leaving Waterdeep? Can the alliance path be attempted retroactively? The tone is operational reassessment, not recrimination.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:302: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone with 1–3 Eyes. The remaining lair heists are available in any order. The Report on Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) points toward **Cassalanter Villa** if not yet completed. The Letter from Nar'l (U3) confirms the BD/Xanathar connection, providing context for **Xanathar's Lair**. Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (if recovered from Scene 5) both point toward **Kolat Towers**. If this was Eye #3 — the Full Awakening — **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:304: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. The caper's resolution carries a different emotional register than **Xanathar's Lair**'s revelatory awakening or **Cassalanter Villa**'s moral reckoning. This quest's aftermath is about relationships — with Jarlaxle (enemy, rival, or ally?), with Zelifarn (the crystal ball decision), with Manshoon's Zhentarim (confirmed threat, now owed payback). The party has engaged with the most charming villain in the Grand Game. What that engagement produced depends entirely on how they played it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:334: - **Fireball! Scene 4** — First visit to Sea Maidens Faire. Nimblewright detector results. Zord's office. Mini-heist aboard the Eyecatcher. Complete and partial nimblewright ledger. Jarlaxle's introduction. All prior party intelligence enters this quest here.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:337: - **Notable Figures** — Full NPC profiles for Jarlaxle, Soluun, Fel'rekt, Krebbyg, Zelifarn, Margo Verida, Khafeyta Murzan, Fenerus Stormcastle, Malcolm Brizzenbright.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:338: - **Bestiary** (not yet drafted) — Custom stat block for Jarlaxle (boss encounter). Stat blocks for Soluun, Fel'rekt, Krebbyg (drow gunslingers).
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:339: - **Bregan D'aerthe** organization page — Bregan D'aerthe faction overview and NPC roster. **Bregan D'aerthe** Factions guide page — outpost entries, response team compositions, escalation framework, Revelation List.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:340: - **Xanathar's Lair** — Stone 1-Eye impressions (ship in harbor, bald elf) point toward this quest. Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist in **Xanathar's Lair** shapes this quest's stakes. Report on Seven Masks Theater (X35) provides third clue path to this quest.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:341: - **Cassalanter Villa** — Report on Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) provides Three Clue Rule path to **Cassalanter Villa**. "Vessa" the BD operative in **Cassalanter Villa** previews Jarlaxle's surveillance operations.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:342: - **Kolat Towers** — Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (Scene 5) both provide independent Three Clue Rule paths to **Kolat Towers**. Combined with Jarlaxle's BD intelligence on Manshoon (alliance/patron path), this is the strongest entry path to **Kolat Towers** in the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:356: The GM should telegraph this genre from the briefing onward. When faction contacts describe Jarlaxle, they should convey that this is not a paranoid beholder in a sealed lair or desperate parents in a gilded villa. This is a man who enjoys being robbed if the thieves are interesting enough. That single characterization reframes the entire arc.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:360: This quest is the only lair heist that offers three fundamentally different approaches to obtaining the Eye. This is because Jarlaxle is the only villain who would willingly trade it for something he values more. Xanathar does not negotiate — it eats envoys. The Cassalanters are too desperate — the children's deadline consumes their attention. Manshoon holds no Eye. Jarlaxle is the one Grand Game villain who treats every interaction as a transaction, and the Eye is currency he is willing to spend if the price is right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:362: The heist path is the default and receives the most structural support. The alliance path exists because it reflects Jarlaxle's actual priorities — Luskan's Alliance bid matters more to him than one component of a treasure-hunting artifact. The patron path exists because the campaign supports BD membership from **Trollskull Alley** onward and must reward that investment with a meaningful payoff.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:366: ## Jarlaxle as Charming Antagonist
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:368: The Jarlaxle confrontation is the design principle that most distinguishes this quest: it plays as a chess match, not a fight.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:370: Xanathar confronted is a lethal puzzle — eye rays, lair actions, raw destructive power. The Cassalanters confronted are a moral crisis — desperate parents, infernal contracts, children between the party and the door. Jarlaxle confronted is a conversation. He is faster, better informed, better equipped, and more experienced than the party. He also does not want to fight them. He wants to understand them, evaluate them, and determine whether they are more useful as allies than as enemies.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:372: Writing Jarlaxle's confrontation correctly means the GM must play him as someone who is genuinely delighted to discover that the party is competent. His respect, his offers, and his real-time recalculation of whether alliance or opposition serves him — all of it is genuine. The party can influence that calculus through what they say and how they say it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:374: Combat with Jarlaxle is a narrative failure state: the caper's improvisational energy collapsed into violence, and both sides lost something. The text makes this clear without forbidding it. Some parties will fight Jarlaxle. The consequences should reflect that this was a missed opportunity, not a tactical error.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:378: The Zardoz Betrayal Pitch is the single most Jarlaxle element in the arc — a con within a con, a test disguised as a job, a moment where the party discovers that they have been working for the person they are trying to rob. It is conditional because it requires either a BD channel (a PC at Initiate or Soldier rank) or Jarlaxle's independent interest in the party from prior interactions — and because it should feel like a natural development from the party's history, not a forced encounter.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:380: When it fires, the Pitch adds dramatic irony: the party is executing a plan provided by their target, with the target watching through the crystal ball network. Everything Jarlaxle provided is accurate — the floor plans, the crew schedules, the fee. The details are accurate. The frame is the con.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:382: The reveal — Jarlaxle dropping the Nevercott disguise and explaining — should be played for its full dramatic weight. This is one of the campaign's signature NPC moments: the mastermind showing his hand because he has already won, and winning gracefully is more important to him than winning invisibly.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:386: Every lair heist needs a parallel heist actor: **Xanathar's Lair** has Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist, **Cassalanter Villa** has Esvele Rosznar, this quest has Manshoon's Zhentarim strike team. The parallel actor creates three-way pressure, provides a pressure-relief valve, and connects the current quest to future quests.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:388: Manshoon is the correct choice for this quest because: he has the intelligence resources (scrying, double agents) to know the Eye's location; his Zhentarim are the most violent faction, creating genuine danger contrast against Jarlaxle's charm; the strike team's pass-amulets to Kolat Towers provide a Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**; and on the alliance or patron path, the Zhent attack gives Jarlaxle a personal reason to send the party after Manshoon — transforming a clean diplomatic resolution into one that carries a new obligation and provides the strongest possible **Kolat Towers** entry.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:390: The strike always fires regardless of which resolution path the party chose. Manshoon's intelligence is independent of the party's relationship with Jarlaxle. This is a deliberate departure from **Cassalanter Villa**'s Esvele matrix, where the parallel actor could be avoided entirely — Manshoon is more aggressive and more consequential.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:394: Every lair heist has a moral dimension: **Xanathar's Lair** has the smokepowder choice, **Cassalanter Villa** has the children's fate and the four resolution paths. This quest's moral dimension is smaller in scope but personal — the Nimblewright Crystal Ball in U4 belonged to Zelifarn's mother, killed by Jarlaxle's agents.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:396: The choice is whether to return the crystal ball to Zelifarn (honoring a grieving dragon's claim, destroying Jarlaxle's surveillance network) or keep it (gaining a city-wide intelligence tool).
