The Eye is separate — the party can take the Eye regardless. This makes the crystal ball decision purely moral, not mechanically necessary.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:404: The fleet structure also gives the departure deadline physical reality. The ships can leave. If the party boards during a parade window and the parade ends early, the pier fills with returning crew. If the party is in the submarine when Jarlaxle decides to flee, the submarine detaches and submerges — taking the party with it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:408: If this quest delivers the third Eye, this is the campaign's pivotal revelation. The Stone delivers its revelation as riddle and impression, not as an address: the Brandath Crypts, a patient and unnamed guardian, imagery requiring interpretation rather than a key. The command phrase is not spoken — that knowledge awaits the ceremonial ritual in **Vault of Dragons** Scene 3. This moment must be given its full weight regardless of which quest delivers it — the text frames the Full Awakening identically whether the third Eye came from Xanathar's fishbowl, the Asmodeus statue, or Jarlaxle's crystal ball pedestal.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:426: **To Kolat Towers.** Two independent paths from this quest: Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) references Manshoon's operations and Kolat Towers; and the Zhentarim pass-amulets recovered from the Manshoon strike team (Scene 5) provide physical access to Kolat Towers. These join the existing paths from **Xanathar's Lair** (Samara's testimony from X7, Nihiloor's files from X23) and **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game from C6 or C22). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied to **Kolat Towers** through any combination of prior quests.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:430: **Lords' Alliance.** Laeral Silverhand's specific request — surfaced through Jalester during Scene 1's briefing — is documentation of the Sea Maidens Faire's private cargo manifests, not solely intelligence on Jarlaxle's identity. If Jarlaxle has not yet been unmasked, this frames the Faire as an intelligence target alongside the Eye; if he has been unmasked, she wants to understand what Bregan D'aerthe has been moving through Waterdeep's harbor under diplomatic cover. The cargo manifests are in the Scarlet Marpenoth's captain's office (SM3); recovering them satisfies this hook independent of which path the party takes to the Eye. Full mission details: the Quest Hooks section of the **Lords' Alliance** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:45: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle's interest in Kolat Towers is personal. If **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Manshoon parallel strike fired — and it always fires — Manshoon sent agents to attack Jarlaxle's ships in his own harbor. The experience left Jarlaxle more annoyed than alarmed, which for him is the more dangerous emotional state. "They came to my ship. In my harbor. I want to know where they came from, and I want it to stop." BD intelligence on Kolat Towers is ground-floor-only — the Bregan D'aerthe never penetrated past the second floor of the towers and have no information on the sanctum. What Jarlaxle provides: partial interior layout (K1 through K9), confirmation of the pass-amulet system by name and mechanism, confirmation that Sidra Romeir commands internal security, and the observation that Manshoon's agents carry amulets minted with a specific design that distinguishes them from amulets issued to the towers' service staff. If the party recovered pass-amulets from the defeated Zhentarim strike team during **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Scene 5, Jarlaxle confirms they work — those amulets bypass the force field and unlock the front door. At renown 10+, Jarlaxle provides one additional piece: "The wizard in those towers lost an arm in Undermountain. Whatever he is — and I have suspicions — he came up from below." The Halaster connection is stated without elaboration. It is the first hint that Manshoon's identity is stranger than a Zhentarim faction leader.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:63: - Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (**Sea Maidens Faire**, J30): references Manshoon's Splinter operations.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:118: Jarlaxle's position and the simulacrum's state are predecided by escalation tier. At Unaware and Suspicious, Manshoon is studying in E13 and the simulacrum is dormant in E7. At Alert and Lockdown, Manshoon is in E12 with the staff of power in hand and the simulacrum is activated and standing in E1. These positions do not change during Scene 4 unless the party's actions force Manshoon to move — and Manshoon moves toward a confrontation, not away from one.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:249: **Manshoon's Spellbook (E13 shelves, guarded by helmed horror).** A 9th-level wizard's working spellbook — 42 spells across all schools, with marginalia indicating active research into planar travel and gate mechanics. Vajra offers 15,000 gp and a favor from the Blackstaff's office in exchange for the spellbook, primarily for what its marginalia reveals about Manshoon's research trajectory. The Doom Raiders offer 8,000 gp and priority intelligence access. Jarlaxle offers, characteristically, "a conversation about the future" rather than a price — he is interested in the planar travel research for reasons he does not specify.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:277: **Jarlaxle (if contacted in Scene 1).** If the Manshoon parallel strike fired in **Sea Maidens Faire** and the party helped or acknowledged it: "Now we're even. Or close enough." Jarlaxle wants to read the Report on the Grand Game — specifically Manshoon's analysis of BD's position in the Grand Game, which he suspects is more accurate than anyone outside BD has any right to be. If the party assembled the full Manshoon Identity Reveal: Jarlaxle confirms the one piece he withheld from his Scene 1 briefing. "I said I had suspicions. The arm was one. There is another thing about him — the way he holds the staff. No one born to a wand holds a staff like that. You hold it like something that has always been yours." He suspected. He wanted to see if the party could find the rest.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:317: - **Sea Maidens Faire**, J30 (Jarlaxle's Report) — references Manshoon's operations. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:318: - **Sea Maidens Faire**, Scene 5 (Zhentarim Strike Team) — pass-amulets recoverable from defeated Zhentarim. Entry method for **Kolat Towers**. Jarlaxle's "They came to my ship" motivation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:363: The three-quest reveal structure — Name in **Faction Outposts**, Identity in **Kolat Towers**, Motivation in **Vault of Dragons** — treats Manshoon differently than the other three villain faction leaders. Xanathar is immediately legible; Jarlaxle makes himself legible on his own terms; the Cassalanters are legible once the party reaches their temple. Manshoon is the one faction leader whose true nature requires assembly from distributed evidence, because his true nature is genuinely strange — a clone of a 200-year-old wizard, not just a Zhentarim leader — and because understanding it changes the party's strategic relationship with him in a way that understanding the other faction leaders does not.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:38: | BD operational? (Jarlaxle relationship) | **Sea Maidens Faire** Scene 6 | Allied / Impressed / Robbed / Hostile / Patron |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:55: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. If the party completed Bregan D'aerthe Mission 6 ("The Dive"), Jarlaxle arrives at this conversation already positioned — that mission concluded the BD operational arc leading into **Vault of Dragons**, and he treats this meeting as its payoff rather than its starting point. Jarlaxle's business here is the clearest of any contact's: he wants the dragonstaff of Ahghairon, and he has a specific plan for the gold. At Dread Lord renown, he proposes returning the entire recovered hoard publicly, in the party's name, to Laeral Silverhand — in exchange for a letter of introduction to the Lords' Alliance council on Luskan's behalf. "I get what I actually came here for. You get credit for the single largest act of civic generosity in a century. Everyone is happy except the people who wanted to keep it." If the party's relationship with Jarlaxle is Allied or Patron, he treats this as a genuine partnership and will fight alongside the party at the vault if a hostile faction shows up. If Hostile, he does not appear in Scene 1 at all — his interest in the staff has not gone away, only his willingness to negotiate for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:117: - **A dragon scale.** Type doesn't matter. A specialty shop can supply one for roughly 900 gp. The party may also recall specific sources encountered earlier in the campaign — Zelifarn's collection, Jarlaxle's private quarters, the Cassalanters' study — any of which could yield one for the asking, a favor, or a theft, depending on the party's standing with that source.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:201: | Bregan D'aerthe | Hostile | Jarlaxle with drow gunslinger lieutenants | Seize the dragonstaff; deny the gold to the city |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:202: | Bregan D'aerthe | Allied / Patron | Jarlaxle with drow gunslinger lieutenants | Assist the party; claim the staff through the agreed Scene 1 arrangement |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:203: | Bregan D'aerthe | Impressed | Jarlaxle with one or two drow companions, observing from a distance and not engaging | No hostile intent; Jarlaxle watches the confrontation play out and may approach the party for a conversation once it resolves |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:204: | Bregan D'aerthe | Robbed | If BD has identified the party as the thieves: Jarlaxle with drow gunslinger lieutenants, treating this as Hostile — personal grievance rather than faction interest. If BD has not identified the thieves: Jarlaxle absent. GM determines identification from how the **Sea Maidens Faire** heist resolved and whether the party left evidence. | Reckon with the party over the **Sea Maidens Faire** heist, or no presence at all if the thieves were never identified |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:218: *The Dragonstaff's Stakes.* Whoever holds the dragonstaff of Ahghairon after Aurinax's oath is discharged decides which dragons the ward admits, one touched creature at a time. This is not a minor detail to gloss past in Scene 6's gold logistics — it is the reason Jarlaxle wants it, the reason Manshoon would take it if he could, and the reason Dagult, whatever else he's lost, still believes he has some claim on it. If the party is deciding what to do with the staff, this is the moment that decision's stakes should be made explicit to them.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:264: **Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe, if contacted).** If his Scene 1 proposal was honored — the gold returned publicly in the party's name, the staff or a letter of introduction in his hand — he is, by his own admission, satisfied in a way he does not often permit himself to be. "I came to this city for information. I am leaving with considerably more than that." He does not say goodbye. He simply is not there the next time the party looks.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:266: If Jarlaxle was Impressed — the party made off with Eye #3 and faced him without turning the confrontation hostile — he does not press for the gold or the staff. He congratulates the party on the vault's resolution and offers a remark of genuine admiration before departing. No negative faction consequences follow; BD's posture toward the party improves. He will contact them later with a business proposition, or simply note, through an intermediary, that he is watching their careers with interest.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:268: If Jarlaxle was Robbed and arrived hostile at Scene 5, he accepts that this venture is concluded and does not pursue further conflict over the **Sea Maidens Faire** heist — the Stone is gone, the vault is open, and prolonged vendetta is not his style. But he remembers. Future BD interactions carry the **Sea Maidens Faire** heist as background context: he is not an enemy, but he is no longer neutral, and the asymmetry is permanent. If BD had not identified the thieves by **Vault of Dragons** and Jarlaxle was absent from Scene 5, the **Sea Maidens Faire** theft remains a closed matter with no **Vault of Dragons** consequence — the debt, as far as either party knows, does not exist.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:327: - **Notable Figures** — Existing profiles used directly: Aurinax, Renaer Neverember, Hlam, Sir Ambrose Everdawn, Laeral Silverhand, Mirt, Jalester Silvermane, Vajra Safahr, Savra Belabranta, Jarlaxle, Manshoon. New profile added: Dagult Neverember.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:365: **Xanathar's Lair** through **Kolat Towers** each calibrate their difficulty against a single lair's escalation tier — how aggressively the party moved through **Faction Outposts**'s outposts, essentially. **Vault of Dragons** has no equivalent fresh escalation clock, because it doesn't need one: the Faction-State Summary Table already carries every difficulty variable the campaign has generated across its entire length. A party that dismantled Manshoon's Splinter in **Kolat Towers**, redeemed the Cassalanters in **Cassalanter Villa**, and allied with Jarlaxle in **Sea Maidens Faire** arrives at Scene 5 facing, essentially, nobody. A party that skipped **Kolat Towers**, left the Cassalanters Active-Adversarial, and made an enemy of Bregan D'aerthe arrives facing all three at once. This is not a punishment mechanic bolted onto the finale — it is the entire campaign's accumulated weight, made visible in a single afternoon, which is exactly what a convergence quest should do.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:86: - Drow: will encounter Jarlaxle and Bregan D'aerthe; Jarlaxle has complicated feelings about surface drow; coordinate with DM before session one
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:315: - One exception: Bregan D'aerthe cannot be chosen at session zero. Jarlaxle enters the campaign only if the players follow the nimblewright investigation in Arc C, which leads to the Sea Maidens Faire. If that thread is never followed, Bregan D'aerthe remains adversary or neutral. Interested players should know this before getting their heart set on it.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:328: | Bregan D'aerthe | Jarlaxle (as "Zardoz Zord") | Best intelligence in the city; drow resources | Loyalty — and eventually, a political favor for Luskan |
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:330: - Bregan D'aerthe conditional entry note: this faction is only available if the players follow the nimblewright investigation to the Sea Maidens Faire in Arc C. Discuss with any interested player now so they understand it is a path to unlock, not a session zero pick.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:331: - Conflicting loyalty note: a party split between the Doom Raiders and Bregan D'aerthe restructures Arc H significantly. This is manageable but requires a table conversation before it happens.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:337: 1. **Bregan D'aerthe conditional entry:** The chapter must make unmistakably clear that Bregan D'aerthe is not a session zero faction choice. The conditional entry mechanic (Arc C nimblewright → Sea Maidens Faire → Jarlaxle contact) should be explained with enough specificity that a player understands what they need to do in play to unlock it — without spoiling Arc C's investigation.

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:98: *Era 3 — Bregan D'aerthe's Long Road (decades before the campaign)*
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:99: - Jarlaxle Baenre born illegitimate in Menzoberranzan's most powerful house; should not have survived infancy; what it says about him that he did
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:100: - The founding of Bregan D'aerthe ("those of the broken house"): a mercenary company built from drow males without house ties — no status, no future underground, nothing to lose; how Jarlaxle turned disposability into leverage
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:102: - The Luskan operation: Jarlaxle as the hidden power behind the Ship Captains council for years; his goal throughout — Lords' Alliance membership for Luskan — explained here as the destination he has been moving toward the entire time
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:103: - This era ends with Jarlaxle's intelligence about Neverember's vault reaching him; his decision to move on Waterdeep; the Sea Maidens Faire established as the vehicle
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:133: - The convergence: all four factions move on Waterdeep in the same window — Manshoon's cell establishing Kolat Towers, Jarlaxle arriving with the Sea Maidens Faire, the Cassalanters accelerating their timeline, the Xanathar Guild responding to new pressure from competitors it hasn't yet fully identified
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:134: - Nar'l Xibrindas placed inside the Xanathar Guild by Jarlaxle before the campaign begins; intelligence about the Stone and the vault already flowing to Bregan D'aerthe

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:154: - **Informants**: the Watch has informants in every ward; every faction has informants in the Watch; Jarlaxle has informants in every other faction; Skeemo Weirdbottle compiles all of it
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:170: | Bregan D'aerthe | Full intelligence via Nar'l Xibrindas inside Xanathar Guild | No Eye yet | Moving to acquire one; observing all other factions |

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:302: ### Jarlaxle Baenre
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:306: > **Conditional Entry:** Jarlaxle enters the Grand Game as a named player only if the nimblewright investigation (Arc C) leads to the Sea Maidens Faire. If the PCs never follow that thread, Bregan D'aerthe remains a background faction. Personality phases below apply only if the conditional is met. See: Ch. 1 Faction Affiliations; Key Decisions, item 4 below.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:312: *Phase 1 (universal) — Zardoz Zord*

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:359: 1. **Lords' Alliance Membership for Luskan** — the real prize; Bregan D'aerthe's Luskan operations need political legitimacy; method: trade intelligence and an Eye to the PCs or directly to the Open Lord; contingency: if the avenue closes, Bregan D'aerthe continues without it — Jarlaxle loses a round, not the game; connects to Arc J political aftermath
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:361: 3. **Waterdeep as Permanent Base** — the Sea Maidens Faire is seasonal; Jarlaxle wants Bregan D'aerthe with a permanent Dock Ward presence; method: be indispensable to whoever resolves the vault situation; this is the long play that outlasts Dragon Heist
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:363: **Key design note:** Jarlaxle is the only villain whose faction (Bregan D'aerthe) the PCs can join. This must be written directly into the campaign structure, not treated as an edge case. A party that joins Bregan D'aerthe inverts Arc H — the Faire becomes their base, and Jarlaxle sends them after another faction's Eye.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:377: 4. **Jarlaxle's conditional entry backup:** If the group is unlikely to follow the nimblewright investigation in Arc C, the DM should create a second clue path to the Sea Maidens Faire. The conditional should not become a missed arc because of one failed investigation thread. The Three Clue Rule applies here as it does everywhere.

campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:177: Each faction reaches these tiers at different paces. Xanathar escalates faster and more irrationally. Jarlaxle almost never reaches tier 4 without choosing to. The Cassalanters prefer tier 3 indefinitely — the less visible their pursuit, the better.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:184: - Jarlaxle and Xanathar: managed tension; Nar'l keeps Xanathar occupied with curated intelligence; Jarlaxle's goal is a predictable beholder, not a dead one
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:185: - Jarlaxle and Manshoon: fragile non-interference; each considers the other the only genuine peer in the game; neither wants direct conflict because both would rather the other faction absorb the other factions' attention
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:194: - What a faction wants in exchange for standing down (Doom Raiders: information; Manshoon: an asset or a demonstration of usefulness; Cassalanters: discretion; Bregan D'aerthe: the political favor they've always wanted)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:220: *The unique faction: Bregan D'aerthe*
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:222: Bregan D'aerthe is the only villain faction PCs can join. This changes Arc H's structure entirely — the Sea Maidens Faire becomes a home base, and Jarlaxle sends the party after another faction's Eye rather than being the target of a heist. The arc's inversion is the point: PCs working for Jarlaxle experience the same heist framework from the other side.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:224: Bregan D'aerthe is available only if the nimblewright investigation (Arc C) leads to the Sea Maidens Faire and the PCs respond to Jarlaxle's approach by negotiating rather than stealing. PCs who join take on Jarlaxle's goal (Lords' Alliance membership for Luskan) as their own faction obligation, which comes due in Arc J.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:228: PCs in different factions will have conflicting faction interests by design. The Lords' Alliance wants the gold returned; the Doom Raiders want Manshoon destroyed; Force Grey wants everything kept quiet; Bregan D'aerthe wants a political favor. This is the campaign's social texture. The DM should let faction loyalties create real pressure on individual PCs without forcing party fragmentation.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:266: *Why Jarlaxle's Entry Is Conditional*
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:268: The design note defends the conditional entry mechanic — Jarlaxle enters the Grand Game as a named player only if the nimblewright investigation reaches the Sea Maidens Faire. This is argued on player agency grounds: if Jarlaxle is always present and always relevant, he dominates the campaign's tone; his entry being conditional means groups that follow the thread get the full Bregan D'aerthe arc, while groups that don't still have three complete villain arcs. The note also covers the backup obligation: the Three Clue Rule requires at least three independent paths to the Sea Maidens Faire, and at least one must be passive (requiring no active player investigation to trigger). A DM who finds the group is unlikely to follow the nimblewright thread is expected to introduce the backup path rather than let the arc disappear.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:312: - Arc C: Three Clue Rule under pressure (fireball investigation); faction response teams all deploy; Jarlaxle's conditional entry gates here
